### `substr(C,int,int)` position-zero parity fix (2026-10-02)

- Added `substrMatchesSparkForNegativeZeroAndLongRanges`, comparing schema and full projected rows against Spark for negative, zero, and positive starts, an overlong range, empty strings, and NULL. The first Docker run exposed a concrete difference: for `"abcdef"`, Spark returns `"abc"` at position `0`, length `3`, while DuckDB's raw `substring` returned `"ab"`. The adapter now normalizes only position zero to start at one; the rerun passes all fixture cases.
- The corrected Docker reactor used the canonical pinned capsule; `Zingg07ExactCompileChecks` passed **3/3**, the Spark differential **1/1**, zero failures/errors/skips, `BUILD SUCCESS`. Surefire reported **3,936.0 s** and the reactor **1:08 h**, an anomalously slow local Docker/WSL run; this is recorded as a test-runtime warning, not a benchmark claim. The generated matrix now records **57/93 dedicated**, **36/93 selected-but-unproven**. Full regex/string edge parity remains open. Validation was Docker-only.

### Docker package smoke coverage added (2026-09-29)

- Added `packaging/verify-docker-package.sh` and wired it into the Linux offline-package CI lane after fresh package assembly and verification. The smoke runs the actual bundled Linux launcher/JRE/worker in `ubuntu:24.04`, without network or system Java, with a read-only root and bundle mount and temporary writable `/tmp`; it checks request-correlated `ping`/`shutdown` output and process success, and prints the resolved container image ID/repository digest. The workflow regression test passes; shell syntax passes; packaging suite passes on WSL (**90 tests, 1 skipped**) and Windows (**90 tests, 10 skipped**).
- The first local Docker attempt exposed that Docker's default tmpfs mount prevented executable mapping of DuckDB's extracted JNI library (`UnsatisfiedLinkError: failed to map segment from shared object`). The test now gives only temporary `/tmp` an `exec` mount; the container root/package remain read-only and the network remains disabled. A fresh package built from the current tree then passed `DOCKER_PACKAGE_SMOKE_SUCCESS` on Docker Desktop Engine 29.8.1, Linux/amd64. Ubuntu image ID/repository digest: `sha256:008173c23f95b170204355c12626cb5a965d779a7e1283b09e9cffbb1bf33ca3`; bundle `SHA256SUMS` digest: `4561f56b170a5c3ee2341eafc4ac4c86afc1544ed60144515e9ca74dba9d4c15`; worker JAR digest: `78781a675fd25345a4f8f3920edc06b8fce1e7c10e8f249e187c4271f27804eb`. Container returned the expected correlated ping/shutdown responses with exit code zero. The CI script and test tolerate an explicit Windows Docker CLI path (`DOCKER_CLI`) for local WSL/Desktop invocation while defaulting to `docker` on hosted Linux. No `Dockerfile`/Compose-based image product is implied; this is a clean-container smoke test of the distributable package. Hosted Linux execution remains pending.

### Request cancellation race regression (2026-09-29)

- Added a worker-protocol regression that submits 32 `ping` requests with immediate matching `cancel` controls, then checks all 65 correlated responses (including shutdown): every request/cancel ID occurs exactly once, the target either completes normally or returns the cancellation diagnostic consistently with its acknowledgement, and the worker exits cleanly. The focused `runtime-worker -am` reactor passed on Windows (**4/4 worker tests**) and WSL2 (**4/4**). The full Python client suite also passed on Windows (**17 tests, 7 subtests**) and WSL (**17 tests, 7 subtests**); WSL's installed pytest emitted one unrelated unknown-config-option warning. One initial assertion used the wrong diagnostic wording; the observed contract is `operation cancelled`, and the corrected regression passes on both systems. Together with the separately passing queue-overflow/cancellation test, this resolves the small protocol race/queue slice only. Packaged Arrow cancellation is now separately verified below; durable mutation phases, broad failure cleanup and crash/power-loss recovery remain outstanding.

### Durable label write/cancellation retry race (2026-09-29)

- Extended the packaged-worker Python process suite with 24 cancel-versus-`enqueue_pending_label` attempts against a temporary persistent label store: 18 immediate cancellation races and 6 deterministic completion-before-cancel cases. Each accepts the protocol's `cancel_requested`/`not_active` acknowledgement and either normal completion or `operation cancelled`; the client retries with the identical producer idempotency key, requires the next duplicate submission to report replay, pages the durable queue, and proves all expected pairs exist exactly once. This intentionally checks recovery semantics, not cancellation rollback for a commit that already won.
- The packaged-worker process suite passed **3/3 on Windows and 3/3 on WSL**. The fresh Linux package was run again in the networkless Ubuntu 24.04 + Python 3.12-slim Docker validation; all **3/3 tests passed inside the container**, including the 24 durable races and the prior Arrow `run_v2` cancellation test. Image and bundle digests are unchanged from the preceding Docker evidence. The full Python suite then passed **19/19 on Windows and 19/19 on WSL** with warnings treated as errors in WSL; other durable/model/output cancellation points and crash-recovery behavior remain open.

### Cancellation-aware atomic model publication (2026-09-29)

- `ModelArtifactWriter` now accepts a request-scoped cancellation predicate and checks it before staging and at atomic publication boundaries. If cancellation arrives after moving the existing artifact to backup, or after renaming the staged artifact into place but before final verification/commit, its rollback removes the staged/new tree and atomically restores the prior artifact. Native classifier and histogram training pass the active `DuckCancellation` token into the writer; the worker reports the correlated `operation cancelled` result when that token wins.
- Added deterministic fault-injection coverage for cancellation at both atomic-move boundaries, verifying the old sentinel artifact is restored, the stage and backup are removed, and cancellation remains the primary failure. Focused seven-module Maven reactor passed on Windows (**ModelFormatTest 19 run / 0 failures / 2 capability skips; NativeClassifierTrainerTest 7/7; ConfiguredWorkerServerTest 5/5**) and WSL (**19/19, 7/7, 5/5; zero skips**), each with `BUILD SUCCESS`. This proves publisher rollback boundaries and token plumbing compile/test locally; an end-to-end packaged worker cancellation precisely during model rename, and the commit-wins branch after final verification, remain to be exercised.

Fixture-intake validation on 2026-09-25 (integrity gate strengthened 2026-09-28):
the checked-in upstream Zingg model fixture contains 45 manifest entries;
`Zingg07FixtureIntakeTest` hashes every file, including hidden Spark `.crc`
sidecars, from the source tree and verifies exact file-set coverage. It rejects
unsafe/duplicate paths and linked/missing entries while requiring consumable
resources on the classpath. The test confirms VectorAssembler ordering metadata,
PolynomialExpansion degree 3, LogisticRegression threshold 0.4, reads the
real coefficient Parquet through DuckDB JDBC, and imports the fixture into the
native model envelope, reloads it through `ModelReader`, and scores a zero
feature vector while preserving the threshold and polynomial metadata. Maven
still excludes hidden `.crc` files from the test classpath, but the filesystem
hash gate covers them. The current fixture class has **5 tests**. This is not a
Spark 3.0.1 differential or production importer gate.

Spark oracle generation: WSL PySpark **3.5.5** successfully loaded the checked-in
upstream classifier model and produced `spark-differential.json` for zero, one, and ramp
feature vectors. The Java fixture test compares the native classifier importer against the
Spark probabilities for zero, one, and ramp vectors; the latest report for this class has **5/5** passing.
This is a useful local differential, but it is not yet an authoritative Spark
3.0.1/v0.7 comparison because the fixture metadata identifies Spark 3.0.1.

Polynomial ordering correction: the ramp differential initially exposed a real
ordering defect. `PolynomialFeatures` now follows Spark's exponent enumeration
(highest variable grouped by power, then lower-variable exponents), rather than
global degree grouping. Focused compatibility and fixture tests pass after the
correction, including the ramp probability comparison.

Current build-plan progress: **37 of 53** tracked checklist tasks are complete (**69.8% by task count**). The 2026-09-28 blocking-tree audit reopened a previously checked item: the pinned candidate's serialized package names do not match the pinned source namespaces, and the importer fails closed. A 2026-09-29 upstream Git-object audit found the exact `zingg.block` bytes in v0.3.4 test resources and confirmed they were carried into the pinned v0.7 test tree; this establishes historical repository presence, not v0.7 producer version, maintainer authority, or compatible serialization. The 2026-09-27 audit had already reopened the incomplete `Frame`-versus-`ZFrame` coverage claim; the model-backed workflow exists locally but full Zingg phase/model parity remains open. This is a checklist count, not a release-readiness percentage; the other checked gates have not all been independently re-audited.

Model artifact JSON hardening (2026-09-28): the prior provenance fix exposed that `manifest.json` still used an incomplete manual encoder and regex reader, which could lose escaped feature names and accept malformed/duplicate structure. Replaced that path with a shared dependency-free JSON codec for the exact flat artifact schema (string fields plus a string-array `features`). Both writers fully escape U+0000–U+001F and reject unpaired UTF-16 surrogates; both readers reject malformed UTF-8/JSON, duplicate/unknown/missing keys, wrong value types, invalid escapes/surrogates, and validate the provenance timestamp and checksum. Tests round-trip all 32 control characters, quotes, backslashes, Unicode snowman/supplementary characters, and feature strings; negative tests cover trailing syntax, duplicate/unknown keys, wrong feature type, bad escapes, raw controls, invalid surrogate escapes, malformed UTF-8 and no partial publication. The first full reactor exposed a downstream defect the old regex readers had hidden: classifier payload feature SQL containing quotes was escaped differently from the imported manifest, causing a false mismatch. Unified classifier payload encoding with the codec and replaced regex-based payload parsing with strict typed parsing and dimension/value validation. Focused `Zingg07FixtureIntakeTest` passed **9/9 on WSL**; the complete Windows `./mvnw.cmd verify -q` passed, with **152 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors, 4 skipped** in 26 Surefire reports. Focused model-format tests also passed on Windows and WSL. `git diff --check` passed (Git only emitted existing LF-to-CRLF normalization warnings). This closes the observed JSON round-trip defect, not a top-level workflow/release task; progress remains **37/53 (69.8%)**.

Cross-platform source-archive reproducibility (2026-09-28): PowerShell and POSIX wrappers now invoke the same Python-standard-library writer. It emits a canonical rooted layout, common manifest schema/profile, sorted entries, fixed ZIP timestamps and Unix modes, and stored payloads. Windows and WSL independently generated and verified the source archive with identical SHA-256 `c6e46c4e3d01729e48376cbb5bc85935357b40ac43d17b7c6f7cf80e9514b01b`. The complete packaging unit suite passed on Windows (**28 tests: 23 passed, 5 skipped**) and WSL (**28/28 passed**); Windows skips only symlink cases because this account lacks symlink-creation privilege. These are local packaging checks, not clean hosted release qualification. The source writer and legacy rooted/rootless verifier fixtures are covered; signatures, signed attestations, independent license/security review, and hosted runner qualification remain open.

Packaging CI coverage follow-up (2026-09-28): the main compile matrix now installs Python 3.12 and runs the complete `packaging/tests` suite on every configured runner (Linux amd64/arm64, macOS x86_64/arm64, Windows), rather than relying only on the two Zingg-contract jobs. Added a wrapper smoke test that invokes the native source-archive front end and verifies its output; a PowerShell-versus-POSIX writer integration comparison runs when both tools exist. Local rerun after the addition: WSL **29 passed, 1 skipped** (PowerShell Core absent); Windows **24 passed, 6 skipped** (five symlink-privilege cases and cross-runtime shell/Pwsh comparison unavailable in this container). Workflow YAML parses, Python compileall and Bash syntax checks pass. The earlier independent Windows/WSL archive hash comparison remains the cross-platform byte-equality evidence. This is CI coverage hardening and does not change the global checklist count (**37/53, 69.8%**).

Strict similarity coverage was extended on 2026-09-26: `SimilarityRegistryTest` validates released Jaccard null/empty/case/punctuation and dissimilar-value vectors, verifies that v0.7 `jaro_winkler` delegates to `jaro`, and checks explicit rejection of unsupported names. The focused test passes.

Local phase registration was made explicit on 2026-09-26: `LocalPhaseExecutors` registers only the native `FIND_TRAINING_DATA` and `MATCH` transformations and leaves `TRAIN`/`LINK` rejected until differential contracts exist. `CompatibilityFunctionsTest` verifies the registration boundary and stable unsupported-phase diagnostic.

Native TRAIN orchestration was extended on 2026-09-26: `registerNativeTraining` now registers an explicit Spark-free TRAIN executor that writes the versioned native blocking artifact before returning the cached input frame. Registration coverage passes; full TRAIN parity remains a separate Spark differential gate.

Dependency evidence was refreshed on 2026-09-26 with `./mvnw.cmd -q -pl runtime-worker -am dependency:tree -Dverbose=false`: the runtime-worker graph resolves DuckDB JDBC 1.5.5.1, Arrow 19.0.0, Jackson, FlatBuffers, Netty, SLF4J, and all internal modules. This refreshes the SBOM input but does not constitute a security/CVE sign-off.

Pipeline dispatch was initially changed on 2026-09-26; a later audit found that treating LINK like MATCH was incorrect. The shared path has since been removed: explicit scoring is MATCH-only, and LINK requires its own executor. The compat module regression tests pass, but canonical LINK output remains unimplemented.

`ZinggPipelineTest` now exercises the corrected path end-to-end with a temporary CSV: configured MATCH produces the expected candidate count and output file without a registered generic phase executor.

Python test discovery was hardened on 2026-09-26: repository-wide `pytest` now excludes generated `dist-*` package trees, whose bundled-JRE legal directories can contain Windows-inaccessible WSL symlink targets. The strict Python suite passes **5/5** from the repository root; `bash -n packaging/*.sh` also passes.

The WSL bundled package `dist-native-wsl-jdk21/zingg-duckdb-0.1.0` was rechecked on 2026-09-26: `verify-package.sh ... true` passed with `PACKAGE_VERIFY_SUCCESS`, and `verify-no-system-java.sh` passed with `NO_SYSTEM_JAVA_SMOKE_SUCCESS` while the normal Java locations were removed from `PATH`.

Full local regression rerun on 2026-09-26: `./mvnw.cmd verify -q`, repository-root `python -m pytest -q`, and `bash -n packaging/*.sh` all completed successfully; Python reported **5/5 passed**.

Windows package verification was also rerun on 2026-09-26 against `dist-current/zingg-duckdb-0.1.0` using `packaging/verify-package.ps1`; it returned `PACKAGE_VERIFY_SUCCESS`. This validates the Windows bundle manifest, SBOM, checksums, and worker layout; it does not replace a clean Windows install on an independent runner.

Maven focused-test ergonomics were fixed on 2026-09-26: the parent Surefire configuration now permits reactor modules without the selected test when `-am` is used. `./mvnw.cmd -q -pl compat-zingg07-runtime -am -Dtest=Zingg07FixtureIntakeTest test` now completes successfully.

The opt-in exact-contract compile gate was rerun on 2026-09-26 using the locally built, normalized Zingg v0.7 capsule at `.m2/repository/zingg/zingg-common-client/0.7.0`; the then-named `Zingg07ExactCompileTest` passed. This proves the pinned `ZFrame`, `DSUtil`, and `PipeUtilBase` symbols compile, but does not claim that a complete functional adapter has been implemented.

POSIX packaging update: native shell equivalents now cover source archives, package verification, provenance, SBOM generation, and package assembly. The WSL end-to-end native package now completes with a coherent Ubuntu JDK 21 toolchain.

Native packaging preflight validation: `package-native.sh` now fails deterministically before Maven when the Linux toolchain is not coherent; the WSL probe reports `javac 21 is required; found 17.0.20.1`. This converts a previously opaque compiler failure into an actionable prerequisite message.

POSIX wrapper validation: with `pwsh` intentionally removed from `PATH`, `package.sh` selected the native assembler and reached the same deterministic Java 21 compiler check under WSL.

WSL remediation: installed Ubuntu `openjdk-21-jdk` as root because the default WSL user had no cached sudo credentials. `java`, `javac`, and `jlink` now all report Java 21.

WSL end-to-end evidence: `CREATE_JRE=true bash packaging/package-native.sh dist-native-wsl-jdk21` completed Maven assembly, source archive creation and verification, Java 21 `jlink` runtime creation, package verification, rollback archive creation, and emitted `PACKAGE_NATIVE_SUCCESS`. The generated checksum manifest initially exposed a self-hash defect; both native and PowerShell packagers now exclude `SHA256SUMS` from its own input, and the rerun passed `PACKAGE_VERIFY_SUCCESS`.

Bundled-runtime smoke: the WSL package's embedded Java 21 runtime launched the worker and returned `smoke ok pong` followed by `smoke ok stopping` for the ping/shutdown protocol.

No-system-Java smoke: new `packaging/verify-no-system-java.sh` removes normal Java locations from `PATH` while retaining only the launcher’s required `dirname` utility; the WSL package still returned ping/pong and shutdown successfully (`NO_SYSTEM_JAVA_SMOKE_SUCCESS`).

CI hardening: Unix offline-package jobs now run `verify-package.sh` with the bundled JRE requirement and execute the no-system-Java smoke verifier; the workflow YAML parses successfully after this change.

Windows CI parity: the Windows package job now removes all non-System32 Java locations from `PATH` and drives the bundled `.cmd` launcher through the ping/shutdown protocol, providing the equivalent no-system-Java smoke gate.

Windows local evidence: the existing Windows bundle passed the restricted-`PATH` smoke locally (`WINDOWS_NO_SYSTEM_JAVA_SMOKE_SUCCESS`), returning ping/pong and shutdown responses.

Fresh artifact recheck: the existing WSL package passed `PACKAGE_VERIFY_SUCCESS`, `NO_SYSTEM_JAVA_SMOKE_SUCCESS`, and `SOURCE_ARCHIVE_VERIFY_SUCCESS` after the latest source changes.

POSIX portability hardening: native package creation and verification no longer require GNU `sha256sum` or `find -printf`; Python's standard-library hashing and path traversal now provide the checksum manifest on Linux and standard macOS shells. Existing WSL package verification still passes after the change.

Negative integrity test: a temporary copy of the WSL bundle was modified after packaging; `verify-package.sh` rejected it with `checksum mismatch: README.md`, confirming that the release gate detects post-build tampering.

SBOM improvement: native package assembly now obtains Maven's JSON dependency tree and feeds it into SPDX generation. A WSL probe produced 27 package entries (including transitive artifacts); dependencies without authoritative license metadata remain `NOASSERTION` and still require license/security review.

End-to-end SBOM evidence: rebuilt `dist-native-wsl-sbom` with `CREATE_JRE=true`; Maven assembly succeeded, source archive and JRE creation completed, `PACKAGE_VERIFY_SUCCESS` and `PACKAGE_NATIVE_SUCCESS` were emitted, and the bundled `sbom.spdx.json` contains 27 packages.

SBOM verifier hardening: `verify-package.sh` now validates SPDX-2.3, requires a non-empty package list, and checks required package identity/version fields; the rebuilt WSL package passes this structural gate.

Bundle content contract: the rebuilt WSL distribution contains `packaging/verify-no-system-java.sh` and an SPDX SBOM with 27 packages (`BUNDLE_CONTENT_CONTRACT_SUCCESS`).

Linux benchmark evidence: the bundled WSL runtime completed the 1,000-row/3-repetition smoke benchmark with startup median 21.832 ms, SQL median 2.569 ms, and graph median 93.540 ms; results are stored in `benchmarks/smoke-2026-09-24-wsl.json`.

Benchmark CI hardening: the Ubuntu validation job now saves the JSON benchmark result, validates its schema and positive timing fields, and uploads it as an artifact. The equivalent local Windows benchmark output passed `BENCHMARK_RESULT_VALID`.

Negative SBOM test: replacing the package SBOM with `{}` caused verification to fail with `SBOM must declare SPDX-2.3`, confirming malformed metadata is rejected.

Resumed-goal regression evidence on 2026-09-24: Maven Java suite **54/54 passed** across all 9 modules; Python control-plane suite **5/5 passed** with warnings-as-errors.

Latest regression rerun: `./mvnw.cmd test` completed successfully across all 9 modules (54 tests, zero failures/errors), and the Python suite completed successfully (5 tests, zero failures/errors).

Post-SBOM-change regression rerun on 2026-09-24: Java **54/54 passed** across all 9 modules and Python **5/5 passed** with warnings-as-errors.

Latest Maven lifecycle gate: `./mvnw.cmd verify` completed successfully across all 9 modules on 2026-09-24; all applicable tests passed and the shaded runtime worker was rebuilt.

Resource-budget regression: the new `configuredMemoryAndTempBudgetsAreAppliedAndObservable` test passed in the focused engine-duckdb suite (**5/5**), confirming DuckDB receives and reports the configured 64 MiB memory limit and 16 MiB temporary-directory limit. Independent RSS/spill stress validation remains open.

Expanded engine regression: the complete engine-duckdb test selection passed **24/24**, including ownership, cancellation, frame metadata, graph output, hash registry, resource snapshot, and resource-budget tests.

Expanded engine regression after low-memory stress coverage: **25/25 passed**, including `DuckSpillBudgetTest`.

Latest full-reactor test rerun after low-memory stress coverage: `./mvnw.cmd test` completed successfully across all 9 modules with the full Java suite green (**57/57**, zero failures/errors).

Latest full-reactor test rerun after in-flight cancellation coverage: `./mvnw.cmd test` completed successfully across all 9 modules with **58/58** Java tests passing and zero failures/errors (engine-duckdb: **27/27**, including the cancellation and low-memory resource tests).

Latest full-reactor test rerun after timeout and partial-output coverage: `./mvnw.cmd test` completed successfully across all 9 modules with **60/60** Java tests passing and zero failures/errors (engine-duckdb: **29/29**).

Latest full-reactor test rerun after graph-clustering parity fixture coverage: `./mvnw.cmd test` completed successfully across all 9 modules with **68/68** non-skipped Java tests passing and zero failures/errors (engine-duckdb: **36/36**). The focused WSL spill suite independently passed **2/2**.

Packaging revalidation: Windows `packaging/verify-package.ps1 -RequireJre` passed against `dist-current/zingg-duckdb-0.1.0`; WSL `verify-package.sh ... true` and `verify-no-system-java.sh` both passed (`PACKAGE_VERIFY_SUCCESS`, `NO_SYSTEM_JAVA_SMOKE_SUCCESS`). Hosted macOS/ARM clean-install evidence remains an external release gate.

Release consistency gate: `git diff --check`, all PowerShell packaging scripts parsed successfully, and every POSIX packaging script passed `bash -n` (`POSIX_SYNTAX_SUCCESS`). Windows package verification was rerun successfully after the gate.

Optional Zingg v0.7 gate hardening: the exact compile test now lives in a profile-only test source set. The default reactor remains capsule-independent and passes **67/67** non-skipped Java tests; with `-Dzingg07.contract.profile=true` and the pinned capsule JAR, exact compile plus reflective contract probes pass **2/2**.

Performance evidence: `BenchmarkMain --size 1000 --repetitions 3` now also measures offline Arrow IPC; with DuckDB JDBC 1.5.5.1 and Java 21, medians were startup **84.73 ms**, SQL aggregation **1.92 ms**, graph closure **166.62 ms**, Arrow write **64.64 ms**, and Arrow read/ingest **847.88 ms**. Raw result is stored in `benchmarks/smoke-arrow-2026-09-24.json`; earlier five-repetition baseline remains in `benchmarks/smoke-2026-09-24-current.json`.

Native Image prerequisite check: `packaging/build-native-image.ps1` was invoked on 2026-09-24 and failed deterministically because `native-image`/GraalVM for Java 21 is not installed on this host. The script reports the exact prerequisite and supports an explicit executable override; native-image comparison remains externally gated.

Python strict gate: after adding project-level `pytest` configuration for fixture loop scope and repository import path, `PYTHONWARNINGS=error python -m pytest python/tests -q` passed **5/5**. Windows package verification also passed in the same validation run.

Linux resource-budget evidence: the focused `DuckRuntimeOwnershipTest` passed **5/5** under WSL2 Ubuntu with the native JDK 21 toolchain, confirming the same settings behavior on Linux.

Successful bounded-sort and spill evidence: `DuckSpillBudgetTest.boundedSortCanCompleteWithConfiguredTempBudget` completes a 5-million-row hash-ordered aggregation with one DuckDB thread, a 64 MiB memory limit, and a 512 MiB temporary-directory limit while a concurrent observer detects a non-empty file in the configured DuckDB temporary directory. The focused WSL suite passes **2/2**, including the existing deterministic 16 MiB bounded-failure case. This supplies direct filesystem evidence of a physical spill for the current DuckDB version and query plan; future DuckDB versions still require canary validation.

Independent RSS probe: the bundled WSL worker answered ping, reported **77,570,048 bytes** resident via `/proc/<pid>/statm`, answered shutdown, and exited with code 0. This supplies the independent RSS measurement; forced-spill and budget-stress enforcement remain open.

Repeatable RSS gate: `packaging/verify-rss-linux.sh` now performs the same independent `/proc` measurement and clean shutdown check; it passed under WSL with `RSS_LINUX_SMOKE_SUCCESS` and is wired into Unix offline-package CI. Forced-spill and budget-stress enforcement remain open.

RSS prerequisite diagnostic: the verifier now rejects a package without `runtime/java` before attempting protocol I/O, while the full WSL Java 21 package still passes the RSS smoke.

Low-memory stress evidence: added `DuckSpillBudgetTest`. A 2-million-row hash-ordered aggregation under a 16 MiB DuckDB memory limit deterministically raises `DuckException` (DuckDB reports the 16 MiB pinning limit), and the focused test passes **1/1**. This validates bounded failure behavior; successful forced-spill, cancellation/timeout, and orphan-cleanup gates remain open.

In-flight cancellation evidence: `DuckRuntimeOwnershipTest.inFlightCancellationInterruptsQueryAndKeepsJobUsable` now cancels a running trillion-row query, observes the expected wrapped `DuckException`/DuckDB interrupt, and verifies the same job can execute `select 1` afterward. The focused ownership suite passes **6/6**. This closes the cancellation sub-check only; timeout, process-kill, partial-output, orphan-cleanup, and successful forced-spill behavior remain open.

Scheduled-timeout evidence: `DuckCancellation.cancelAfter(Duration)` now provides a daemon-backed deadline that invokes the same JDBC statement cancellation path. `DuckRuntimeOwnershipTest.scheduledCancellationEnforcesQueryDeadline` interrupts a long-running query at the deadline and verifies the job remains usable; the focused ownership suite passes **7/7**. This closes the in-process timeout sub-check; external worker timeout/kill, partial-output, orphan-cleanup, and successful forced-spill gates remain open.

Partial-output safety evidence: `DuckFrame` exports now write to a sibling `.partial` file, enforce the output budget before publication, atomically replace the destination when possible, and delete the temporary file on failure. `ResourceBudgetTest.rejectedOutputLeavesDestinationAndNoPartialFile` passes **4/4**, proving a rejected CSV export preserves the prior destination and leaves no partial artifact.

Checklist reconciliation: the release-job matrix requirement is now marked complete because `.github/workflows/build.yml` defines Linux amd64/arm64, macOS x86_64/arm64, and Windows x86_64 offline-package jobs with launcher prerequisite checks. Hosted execution remains separately open in the validation checklist.

After connector-policy hardening, a fresh full `./mvnw.cmd test` rerun again passed **54/54** with zero failures or errors across the 9-module reactor.

Connector-policy hardening: configured extension allowlists are now normalized to lowercase at construction as well as at lookup; the focused engine-api suite passes **7/7**, including the new uppercase-allowlist regression test.

This report distinguishes executed evidence from implemented-but-unvalidated scope.

Pinned Zingg v0.7 capsule evidence: `prepare-zingg07-capsule.ps1` cloned `https://github.com/zinggai/zingg.git` at commit `48cb157b4f35fcfa733e2e7f9699ea988e018738`, built `common/client`, normalized the JAR, and produced reproducible SHA-256 `edde9b14598c83e6cad2a06b9ea3e53c8e88ae7b70c3a48c6d247d73b8c737cb`. The opt-in `zingg07-contract` probe passed **1/1** against the local capsule and confirms the required generic contract classes are present. The upstream published POM contains an unresolved `${zingg.version}` transitive dependency, so the probe deliberately loads the hash-verified local JAR directly; this does not yet prove exact adapter compilation or Spark model parity.

Default compatibility regression after adding the opt-in capsule probe: the selected reactor completed successfully; `compat-zingg07-runtime` ran **14 tests with 13 passing and 1 intentionally skipped** when the opt-in capsule property is absent. The probe is therefore isolated from normal Spark-free builds.

Exact-contract compile evidence: with `zingg07.contract.profile=true` and `zingg07.contract.jar` set to the hash-verified local capsule, `Zingg07ContractProbeTest` and the then-named `Zingg07ExactCompileTest` passed **2/2**. The latter compiles imports for `ZFrame`, `DSUtil`, and `PipeUtilBase` against the pinned v0.7 JAR. This is stronger contract evidence, but it is not yet the production adapter or model/phase parity implementation.

Cross-platform documentation update: `packaging/README.md` now provides a Windows PowerShell versus Linux/macOS/WSL command-parity table, explicitly identifies the tested Windows restricted-`PATH` and Ubuntu WSL gates, and separates those local gates from still-unexecuted hosted macOS/ARM qualification.

Post-documentation static/regression checks: `bash -n packaging/*.sh`, workflow YAML parsing, `git diff --check`, and the Python control-plane suite all pass; the Python suite remains **5/5** with warnings treated as errors.

Final local quality-gate rerun after export-safety changes: shell syntax, workflow YAML parsing, `git diff --check`, and Python warnings-as-errors validation all passed; Python remains **5/5**.

WSL Linux regression after timeout/export changes: Ubuntu WSL with JDK 21 ran the focused ownership and resource-budget suites successfully (**11/11**, zero failures/errors), including scheduled cancellation and partial-output cleanup.

Linux process-lifecycle evidence: new `packaging/verify-process-kill-linux.sh` launches the bundled worker in its own process group, verifies ping, sends `SIGTERM`, escalates only on timeout, and scans `/proc` for survivors. The verifier passed against the full WSL Java 21 bundle with `PROCESS_KILL_LINUX_SMOKE_SUCCESS`; it is wired into the Unix CI package gate.

Windows process-lifecycle gate: added `packaging/verify-process-kill-windows.ps1`, which launches the bundled `.cmd` worker, verifies ping, calls `.Kill($true)` on the process tree, and requires termination. The script is wired into the Windows CI package job; local execution is pending a locally available Windows bundle containing `runtime/java` (the existing local bundle intentionally has no bundled JRE and was rejected by the prerequisite check).

Fresh WSL production-package evidence: rebuilt `dist-native-wsl-lifecycle` with `CREATE_JRE=true` and `JAVA_HOME` pointing to JDK 21; `verify-package.sh` returned `PACKAGE_VERIFY_SUCCESS`, and the exact rebuilt bundle returned `PROCESS_KILL_LINUX_SMOKE_SUCCESS`. The native assembler now also includes the Windows lifecycle verifier in the bundle. The Windows verifier itself remains hosted-CI-only until a Windows package with bundled JRE is available locally.

Lifecycle gate static validation: PowerShell AST parsing reports `POWERSHELL_SYNTAX_VALID`; all POSIX scripts pass `bash -n`; the workflow parses as `WORKFLOW_YAML_VALID`; and `git diff --check` passes.

DuckDB compatibility matrix update: `.github/workflows/build.yml` now includes an informational `prequalification-2.0.0` lane alongside the pinned and published canaries. The lane is explicitly non-blocking until a compatible DuckDB 2.0 artifact exists; workflow YAML validation passes.

Executable capability-report evidence: added `io.zingg.duckdb.engine.FrameCapabilityReport`, which emits JSON for every public `Frame` method and its implementation status. The current report is **34/34 implemented**. This closes the backend `Frame` inventory gap, but the broader ZFrame differential matrix and unsupported/capability semantics remain open.

CI enforcement: the Ubuntu validation job now runs `FrameCapabilityReport`, parses its JSON, and requires `implemented == total`; the local gate passed with `FRAME_CAPABILITY_REPORT_SUCCESS 34/34` and workflow YAML parsing remains valid.

Coverage-report scope correction (2026-09-28): renamed the CI assertion to **internal Frame declaration coverage (not ZFrame parity)** and made it assert the reported interface identity. Added a separate validation-job bootstrap of the pinned upstream capsule, followed by the hash-pinned 93-signature verifier and generated adapter-matrix freshness check. Local WSL execution rebuilt commit `48cb157b4f35fcfa733e2e7f9699ea988e018738` in an isolated temporary checkout; canonical capsule SHA-256 matched `a289903fae2ed9ff3a0a3360fed3c80232d70fce848df27e2c6a6ee8406c4b5d`, inventory returned `ZFRAME_INVENTORY_SUCCESS methods=93`, and matrix check returned `ZFRAME_ADAPTER_MATRIX_SUCCESS checked=93`. This proves pinned API inventory and routing-matrix consistency only—not per-overload semantics, approved Zingg goldens, or release parity. Workflow YAML was re-parsed after the change; full Maven `verify` on Windows passed **147 tests, 0 failures/errors, 4 skipped**. The global checklist remains **37/53 (69.8%)**.

| Offline-safe Arrow IPC round trip | `DuckFrameMetadataTest` writes with JDBC/Arrow IPC, validates the stream directly with `ArrowStreamReader`, and reads it through `DuckPipeReader` under DuckDB JDBC 1.5.5.1 and Arrow 19.0.0; full `./mvnw.cmd test` rerun | Primitive, null, multi-batch, date, timestamp, binary, and decimal egress→ingress pass with decimal precision/scale preserved; direct IPC row/schema validation, job-schema qualification, source tagging, and generated row IDs are covered. Unsupported nested JDBC values now fail explicitly without publishing output. Launcher/Surefire module opens remain required; native list/struct support remains open |

## 2026-09-23 audit remediation evidence

The latest audit remediation pass added and verified the following changes:

- Job-slot permits are released when job-schema creation fails.
- Job JDBC connections are closed even when cleanup statements fail.
- SQL frames lazily discover columns, restoring composition for ordinary SQL relations and file-ingress-derived frames.
- Cache materialization evaluates the source plan once before enforcing row limits.
- Python validates response/request correlation and kills a worker that ignores graceful termination.
- Unix package manifest assertions and bundled-JRE executable naming are platform-aware.
- Project/module SBOM license declarations now match the repository AGPL-3.0-only license.
- Regression coverage was added for frame metadata and cache composition.
- Unsupported compatibility phases now fail explicitly instead of silently returning identity frames.
- Versioned worker payloads preserve delimiter-rich paths and SQL expressions without nested `|`/`;` parsing.
- Candidate self-joins now emit deterministic left columns plus `z_`-prefixed right columns; pair-shape regression coverage passes.
- Imported/native classifier scoring now uses the classifier-owned threshold and emits `z_prediction` before filtering.
- Strict-profile Jaccard regression vectors now cover null/empty, case normalization, punctuation normalization, and dissimilar values.
- Strict-profile Jaro is available and `jaro_winkler` deliberately aliases it to preserve the released v0.7 behavior.
- Input, output, and spill budgets are now typed separately; spill limits map to DuckDB's temporary-directory setting instead of a generic file-size check.
- Arbitrary worker diagnostic SQL is disabled by default and requires explicit `--unsafe-debug-sql`; status reports the active mode.
- The obsolete `WorkerServer` implementation, which bypassed the active diagnostic-SQL gate and used delimiter payloads, was removed; the complete Maven suite passed after removal.
- Malformed or non-finite optional classifier numeric fields now fail explicitly instead of silently applying defaults; the targeted compatibility test passed.
- `FrameCapabilityCoverageTest` now provides an executable structural coverage guard for the complete `Frame` API, and the capability matrix documents semantic parity gaps.
- Graph entity scoring now uses a pair-score index instead of rescanning all edges for every component pair; a 301-node chain regression passes.
- Collection now has an independent typed byte budget (`--max-collect-bytes`); targeted row-collection and budget tests pass.
- Worker `status` now reports the effective row, collect, output, and spill budgets; a shaded-JAR smoke test confirmed values `10/20/30/40` and clean shutdown.
- The build plan now distinguishes locally proven model envelope/checksum/version rejection, deterministic pair projection/file-ingress metadata, and structured worker payloads from their still-pending real-Zingg differential gates.
- Python client coverage now verifies that `max_collect_bytes` is forwarded to the default worker command; 5/5 Python tests pass.
- Added the Zingg-shaped string-splitting overload while retaining predicate split semantics; targeted metadata and structural capability tests pass, with differential parity still open.
- Arrow egress experiment: direct Arrow Java 19 `ArrowFileWriter` round-trip was attempted and rejected by the runtime with `UnsupportedOperationException: sun.misc.Unsafe or java.nio.DirectByteBuffer.<init>(long, int) not available`; the experiment was removed, so offline-safe Arrow egress remains an explicit blocker rather than an unverified claim.
- Bundled JRE creation now includes `jdk.unsupported`, the module required by Arrow's Java 21 memory path; Arrow IPC egress still requires a separate supported writer qualification.
- Dedicated JRE module check passed after the update: `create-jre.ps1` generated a Java 21 image containing both `java.base` and `jdk.unsupported`; POSIX script syntax also passed with `bash -n`.
- The strict compatibility profile now persists `jaroWinklerDelegatesToJaro=true` and `profileSemantics=strict-released-quirks` in both Java and packaging manifests; a regression test passes.
- Python distribution boundary remains explicit: the source wheel contains the control-plane package only, while the verified standalone bundle contains the worker JAR and private Java runtime. A plain `pip install` cannot satisfy the no-system-Java contract until a platform-wheel/bundle distribution design is selected.
- RSS observability uses `/proc/self/status` on Linux and standard `tasklist`/`ps` fallbacks on Windows/macOS; the current-platform regression test requires a positive measurement. Cross-platform CI confirmation remains a release gate.
- RSS subprocess fallbacks are bounded by a two-second timeout and forcibly terminated on timeout, preventing diagnostics from blocking the worker.
- GitHub Actions now uses `actions/setup-java@v5` and explicitly checks `test -x ./mvnw` on Unix runners, guarding the previously observed wrapper-permission failure. Hosted-matrix execution remains pending external CI evidence.
- The hosted validation job now pins its Python control-plane runtime to 3.10 through `actions/setup-python@v5`; local Python metadata and tests are aligned with that declared baseline.
- CI now includes a dedicated Python 3.10–3.14 matrix job for syntax and control-plane unit validation; hosted execution remains pending.
- CI validation now explicitly runs module-lock, pure-runtime, source-archive, and bundled-JRE package verification after release assembly; workflow YAML parsing and the equivalent local checks pass.
- CI workflow parity validation also passed locally: all POSIX entry points passed `bash -n`, all packaging PowerShell scripts parsed, the workflow YAML parsed, and `git diff --check` reported no whitespace errors.
- Requirement traceability audit reconciled every remaining unchecked build-plan gate with an executable exit criterion or an `EXT-01`–`EXT-06` external input; no remaining item is treated as complete based solely on local unit-test results.
- Native Image prerequisite probe is now deterministic: `build-native-image.ps1` reports the actionable missing-GraalVM prerequisite when `native-image` is unavailable; the current Windows JDK 21 environment has no GraalVM Native Image tool, so the native-worker build remains externally gated.
- CI release validation now generates and uploads `provenance.json`; local execution emitted the expected `zingg-duckdb-provenance-1` schema, 22 hashed files, and a non-empty worker SHA-256.
- Windows clean-install launcher smoke passed with `PATH` reduced to `System32` (no system Java discoverable): bundled `bin/zingg-duckdb.cmd` returned `pong` and `stopping` with exit code 0.
- Rebuilt `dist-current` after the latest CI/provenance/native-image changes; Maven assembly, runtime-purity, module locks, source archive verification, bundled JRE creation, and package verification all passed, ensuring the distribution reflects current sources.
- Post-rebuild benchmark smoke at size 1000 with 3 repetitions completed successfully: startup median 30.211 ms, SQL median 2.561 ms, and graph median 99.252 ms; this remains observational until release thresholds are approved.
- The post-rebuild benchmark result is now persisted as machine-readable history in `benchmarks/smoke-2026-09-24.json` with runtime and DuckDB provenance.
- The source archive was regenerated after adding the benchmark record and passed `verify-source-archive.ps1`; `git diff --check` also remains clean.
- Source-archive content inspection confirmed that the refreshed ZIP contains both `benchmarks/smoke-2026-09-24.json` and the updated `packaging/build-native-image.ps1`, not merely valid archive structure.
- OWASP Dependency-Check was attempted with corrected Maven argument quoting; analysis could not start because the NVD API requires an API key (`Invalid API Key, length of 0`) and the reactor was not installed for the standalone plugin invocation. No vulnerability or security sign-off is claimed; CI/release owners must provide an NVD key or cached database and run the scan after a reactor install/package.
- Added an opt-in GitHub Actions `security-scan` job gated by repository variable `ENABLE_SECURITY_SCAN=true`; it installs the Maven reactor, passes `secrets.NVD_API_KEY`, runs Dependency-Check with fail-on-error, and uploads the JSON report. YAML parsing passes locally; hosted execution and secret provisioning remain external.
- The security job explicitly enables Dependency-Check aggregate mode so child-module dependencies are included; the workflow assertion and diff check pass locally.
- Predicate partitioning now has the explicit `partitionByPredicate` API name, while `split(Expression)` remains a compatibility alias; the Zingg-shaped string-splitting overload is distinct. Direct matching/remaining-count coverage passes, and the complete 47-test Java suite passes.
- Duplicate phase registrations now fail explicitly instead of silently replacing an executor; this remains covered by the full 48-test Java suite.
- Added low-resource runtime coverage confirming configured `memory_limit`, `max_temp_directory_size`, and thread count are applied and observable through diagnostics; targeted resource tests pass, with the full suite now at 49 tests.
- Added deterministic concurrent-job coverage proving that `maxConcurrentJobs=1` blocks a second job until the first job closes, then releases and reuses the slot successfully.
- Added run-cleanup coverage proving that closing a job removes its run schema and materialized cache before a subsequent job starts.
- Added pre-execution cancellation coverage proving a cancelled token is rejected before SQL execution and the runtime remains reusable afterward.
- Added a native POSIX `packaging/create-jre.sh` path for Java 21 `jlink` runtime creation; Bash syntax validation passed on Windows, while actual Unix `jlink` execution remains a hosted-platform gate.
- POSIX runtime execution was attempted under local WSL: Java 21 is installed, but the system `jlink` alternative resolves to Java 17, so the script correctly rejected the mismatched toolchain; this is recorded as an environment prerequisite, not a script failure.
- Added executable `packaging/package.sh` as the Unix release entry point; its missing-`pwsh` guard was exercised locally and returned the documented exit code 2. Full assembly remains dependent on PowerShell 7 plus a matching Java 21 JDK.
- Unix offline-package CI now invokes `./packaging/package.sh -Output dist -CreateJre` directly; workflow YAML parsing and executable-entrypoint checks pass locally.
- WSL end-to-end `./packaging/package.sh -Output dist-wsl -CreateJre` completed successfully after Windows-PowerShell path conversion: reactor package, purity checks, module locks, source archive verification, `jlink`, and `PACKAGE_VERIFY_SUCCESS` all passed.
- The Unix wrapper now handles both WSL (`wslpath`) and Git Bash (`pwsh.exe` without `wslpath`) path conventions; Bash syntax and executable checks pass.
- Fresh Windows package assembly with bundled Java 21 completed successfully in `dist-current`: Maven package, runtime-purity and module-lock checks, source archive verification, `jlink`, package verification with `-RequireJre`, SHA-256 inventory, and rollback archive generation all passed.
- Fixed source-archive exclusion for named distribution directories such as `dist-current`; the corrected archive completed in 5.8 MB without recursively packaging its own output. A fresh `package.ps1 -Output dist-current -CreateJre` completed Maven assembly, `SOURCE_ARCHIVE_VERIFY_SUCCESS`, `PACKAGE_VERIFY_SUCCESS`, and bundled-JRE creation.
- The rebuilt packaged runtime contains `jdk.unsupported`; bundled-Java protocol smoke returned `pong` and `stopping` with exit code 0.
- Post-package Python validation passed with syntax compilation and all 5 control-plane unit tests under warnings-as-errors.
- Fresh bundled-worker smoke passed using only `dist-current/zingg-duckdb-0.1.0/runtime/java/bin/java.exe`: tab-protocol `ping` returned `pong`, `shutdown` returned `stopping`, and the process exited successfully.
- Fresh packaged Python-client smoke passed from `dist-current/zingg-duckdb-0.1.0/python`: `WorkerClient` auto-discovered the bundled Java/JAR, returned structured `status`, and completed `ping`/`shutdown` without requiring system Java.
- Release-script audit passed: all packaging PowerShell scripts parse successfully, all Bash entry points pass `bash -n`, the current source archive passes `verify-source-archive.ps1`, and the assembled `dist-current/zingg-duckdb-0.1.0` bundle passes `verify-package.ps1 -RequireJre`.
- A fresh benchmark smoke run completed at size 1000 with 3 repetitions: startup median 30.899 ms, SQL median 1.575 ms, and graph median 91.487 ms on the current Windows/JDK 21/DuckDB JDBC 1.5.5.1 environment. This is observational evidence only; release thresholds remain unestablished.
- Post-package benchmark smoke completed at size 1000 with 3 repetitions: startup median 22.426 ms, SQL median 1.067 ms, and graph median 44.085 ms on the rebuilt Windows/JDK 21/DuckDB JDBC 1.5.5.1 environment. This remains observational evidence only; release thresholds remain unestablished.
- Persisted workflow coverage now executes `findTrainingData -> applyLabels -> trainMatch -> restart -> match` against a temporary DuckDB database and verifies the pre-model restart failure path; both tests pass. `TrainingPlan` now bounds the relation before creating its window column, avoiding DuckDB's invalid `arg_min/arg_max` million-row rewrite for the default cap.
- Benchmark regression coverage now rejects runtime/RSS/spill/output threshold breaches, treats a non-zero measurement against a zero baseline as an infinite regression, and preserves benchmark identity checks; all 3 benchmark infrastructure tests pass.
- Backend-native blocking histograms now use a distinct `duckdb-native-0.1` profile, `0.1.0` version, and `BACKEND_BLOCKING_HISTOGRAM` model type; strict `zingg-0.7.0` blocking-tree artifacts remain separately validated. These checks are included in the latest full 60-test Java suite.
- Model loading rejects mixed strict/backend profile, version, and model-type identities; these checks are included in the latest full 60-test Java suite.

These changes reduce confirmed local defects but do not close the strict Zingg v0.7 adapter, real artifact parity, differential Spark harness, or cross-platform CI evidence gates listed below.

### 2026-09-27 phase-dispatch regression

- `ZinggPipeline` now selects exactly one route: an explicit MATCH matcher or a registered phase executor. A registered MATCH result is no longer post-filtered by the pipeline predicate. An explicit matcher is rejected for LINK, and an unregistered LINK fails before input I/O or output publication.
- Added focused tests for registry MATCH, unsupported LINK, explicit-matcher LINK rejection, and a registered LINK executor invoked once. `.\mvnw.cmd -pl compat-zingg07-runtime -am '-Dtest=ZinggPipelineTest' '-Dsurefire.failIfNoSpecifiedTests=false' test -q` passed on Windows; `.\mvnw.cmd verify -q` also passed.
- This is partial P0.1 progress, not a completed LINK implementation. Typed phase/output contracts, canonical LINK graph output, end-to-end workflow semantics, and cross-platform validation remain open. The build-plan count stays 38/53 (71.7%).

### 2026-09-27 persisted-classifier workflow

- `PersistedMatchWorkflow.trainClassifier` now maps accepted positive/negative pair decisions to candidate IDs, derives `z_label` internally, rejects missing/duplicate pair mappings and label truncation, trains a native classifier, and records its artifact. A backend blocking histogram is explicitly rejected as a classifier at restart.
- `restartFromModel` loads the classifier into a newly constructed runtime; `match` scores candidate pairs with that loaded model instead of applying a predicate filter. The focused test demonstrates different accepted labels producing different prediction counts (2 versus 1) after runtime reconstruction. A separate negative test proves an unmatched accepted decision creates no artifact.
- Focused `PersistedMatchWorkflowTest` (4 tests), full `.\mvnw.cmd verify -q`, and `python -m pytest -q` (5 passed) succeeded on Windows. This does **not** prove a separate OS-process restart, durable label-provider state, upstream Zingg v0.7 model parity, graph LINK output, or the complete P0.2 exit criteria. The checklist remains 38/53 (71.7%).

### 2026-09-27 label replay and corruption hardening

- `InMemoryLabelDecisionProvider.applyLabels` is synchronized across conflict checks and result recording. Reusing an idempotency key with a changed schema or decision list now fails rather than silently replaying an unrelated result; a matching retry retains request correlation and does not double-count workflow decisions. `UNKNOWN` no longer prevents a later definitive human decision.
- Added two provider tests and an integration assertion for same-key replay/mutation. A new workflow test tampers with `model.bin` and checks that classifier reload rejects its checksum while leaving the workflow in `CREATED`. These are local failure-path checks, not durable label storage or a release security audit.

### 2026-09-27 opt-in applied-label snapshot

- Added `PersistentLabelDecisionProvider`, an opt-in Java `LabelDecisionProvider` that writes a bounded, SHA-256-verified binary snapshot using a forced temporary file and atomic replacement. On reconstruction it replays accepted requests through the same conflict/idempotency logic; checksum/format damage fails closed rather than starting with empty decisions.
- `CompatibilityRuntime` now accepts an injected label provider. The two focused provider tests passed, including runtime reconstruction, exact replay, conflict persistence, and tamper rejection.
- The snapshot writer now acquires an OS file lock and reloads the latest state under it before applying a new request. A focused two-instance test proves sequential writes from stale instances do not overwrite each other. Concurrent multi-process stress and process-kill recovery are still unqualified.
- Limits: the worker/default runtime still uses `InMemoryLabelDecisionProvider`; pending-label queues are not persisted by this provider. The opt-in snapshot therefore narrows, but does not close, the restart-safe label-state and full workflow gates. Checklist: 38/53 (71.7%).
- Focused `PersistentLabelDecisionProviderTest` (3 tests), full `.\mvnw.cmd verify -q`, and `git diff --check` passed on Windows after the locking change.

### 2026-09-27 worker label protocol and process restart

- The Java worker now accepts `--labels-root` to select `PersistentLabelDecisionProvider` and exposes `apply_labels` with versioned, delimiter-safe request/response fields. The Python `WorkerClient` forwards `labels_root`, and `DuckWorker.apply_labels` handles applied/replay/rejection results. Without the flag, the default remains in-memory.
- `.\mvnw.cmd -pl runtime-worker -am package -q` passed and built the shaded worker JAR; `python -m pytest -q` passed 6/6. `python tools/verify-worker-label-restart.py runtime-worker/target/runtime-worker-0.1.0-SNAPSHOT.jar` returned `WORKER_LABEL_RESTART_SUCCESS` on Windows. The same script returned `WORKER_LABEL_RESTART_SUCCESS` in WSL with OpenJDK 21.0.12.1 and Python 3.12.3. Each run launched and closed two separate worker OS processes and verified persisted replay plus pair conflict.
- These smokes do not prove durable pending-label intake, classifier training with recovered decisions, concurrent process-kill recovery, canonical Zingg phase outputs, or the entire worker-protocol matrix. The full P0.2 gate and checklist remain 38/53 (71.7%).
- Added `run_v2` with explicit matcher fields because the legacy `run` request could carry a model path but no matcher and therefore could not reach classifier scoring. The Python facade selects `run_v2` for configured matching. Rebuilt the shaded JAR (`.\mvnw.cmd -pl runtime-worker -am package -q` passed), ran `python -m pytest -q` (7/7), and extended the separate-process smoke to train a native classifier in worker A and score two candidate pairs from the loaded artifact in worker B. `WORKER_LABEL_RESTART_SUCCESS` passed on both Windows and WSL after this extension. Strict upstream v0.7 parity and label-derived worker TRAIN remain open.
- The full `.\mvnw.cmd verify -q` reactor also passed after the worker/protocol changes; `git diff --check` found no whitespace errors. This is a covered-build result, not proof that all phase or release gates are complete.

### 2026-09-27 recovered-decision TRAIN smoke

- `LabelDecisionProvider.acceptedDecisions()` and provider implementations expose definitive accepted decisions for resumed training. `PersistedMatchWorkflow.restoreAppliedLabels()` reconstructs its training decisions; worker `train_from_labels` generates bounded candidate pairs and trains from those recovered decisions, not from a caller-supplied label column.
- `.\mvnw.cmd -pl runtime-worker -am test -q` and `.\mvnw.cmd -pl runtime-worker -am package -q` passed on the current worktree. `python -m pytest -q` passed **8/8**. The rebuilt JAR passed `tools/verify-worker-label-restart.py` on Windows and WSL: three distinct worker processes applied labels, recovered/trained, and loaded/scored the model, returning `WORKER_LABEL_RESTART_SUCCESS` on each OS.
- The initial smoke checked replay/conflict, artifact path, two output rows, and `z_prediction` presence; the following entry records stronger assertions. Neither version proves a released Spark/Zingg golden, full negative/cleanup qualification, persisted pending-label queues, or LINK. The checklist remains **38/53 (71.7%)**.
- The three-process smoke was strengthened and rerun successfully on Windows and WSL: training without applied labels now must fail without publishing an artifact; the trained classifier manifest type and payload SHA-256 are checked; MATCH must return exactly `(1,3)` and `(2,3)`, required score/prediction columns, finite scores above threshold, and positive predictions. These are native-fixture assertions, not approved Spark/Zingg score goldens or complete negative/cleanup qualification.

### 2026-09-27 persistent pending-label queue

- `PersistentLabelDecisionProvider` now writes a version-2 atomic, checksummed snapshot containing applied decisions, the pending FIFO queue, and idempotent delivery receipts. It continues to read version-1 applied-only snapshots and migrates them on the next write. Nullable left/right payloads are retained across restart.
- The focused `PersistentLabelDecisionProviderTest` suite passed **6/6** on Windows and WSL. It covers FIFO delivery after restart, exact retry without consuming another pair, changed-limit rejection for a reused key, preservation of applied decisions alongside pending state, v1 migration, checksum rejection, and sequential use by two provider instances. The complete Windows `./mvnw.cmd verify -q` passed; `git diff --check` returned exit code 0.
- This is provider-level durability only. The worker protocol cannot yet enqueue or retrieve pending pairs, the default provider remains in-memory, and concurrent-reader/process-kill/acknowledgement/receipt-retention tests are still missing. The checklist remains **38/53 (71.7%)**.

### 2026-09-27 concurrent pending-label delivery

- Two provider objects in the same JVM could previously overlap their `FileChannel.lock()` attempts even though each object synchronized its own methods. A process-wide lock now serializes those attempts before acquiring the cross-process file lock.
- Added a two-thread, two-instance test that consumes 20 queued pairs with distinct delivery keys and verifies the exact 20 IDs, no duplicates, and an empty queue afterward. The focused suite passed **7/7** on Windows and in an isolated WSL rerun; the complete Windows `./mvnw.cmd verify -q` passed. `git diff --check` returned exit code 0. Cross-process concurrency, process kill during snapshot replacement, and worker-level pending-label protocol are still open; the checklist remains **38/53 (71.7%)**.

### 2026-09-27 worker pending-label protocol

- Added opt-in `enqueue_pending_label` and `get_pending_labels` worker operations and Python facade methods. The new snapshot v3 stores producer keys in addition to applied requests, pending pairs, and delivery receipts. Exact producer and delivery retries are stable across restart; changed payload/schema/limit retries fail. v1 and v2 snapshots remain readable and migrate on write.
- The focused provider suite passed **9/9** on Windows and WSL, including v2 migration and producer replay after consumption. `./mvnw.cmd -pl runtime-worker -am package -q` and full `./mvnw.cmd verify -q` passed on Windows. Python `pytest -q` passed **9/9**, and `git diff --check` returned exit code 0.
- The rebuilt worker passed the three-process `tools/verify-worker-label-restart.py` smoke on Windows and WSL (`WORKER_LABEL_RESTART_SUCCESS` on both). It now checks producer replay and changed-key-payload rejection, first-batch delivery, same-key delivery replay in a later process, FIFO second batch, changed-limit rejection, recovered-decision training, and loaded-model MATCH scoring. The protocol caps delivery at 10 pairs and submitted payload fields at 10,000 UTF-16 code units to stay within the line/snapshot encodings.
- Still open: concurrent distinct OS processes, process kill during write/acknowledgement, receipt retention/compaction, default-mode durability, canonical Zingg v0.7 phase/model parity, and approved golden outputs. This does not close the broader build-plan gate; checklist remains **38/53 (71.7%)**.

### 2026-09-27 cross-process labels and lost acknowledgements

- Added `tools/verify-worker-label-concurrency.py`: four simultaneous worker OS processes enqueue 20 unique pairs, four others consume all 20 without loss or duplication, and two simultaneous processes using one delivery key receive the same single pair. It returned `WORKER_LABEL_CONCURRENCY_SUCCESS` on Windows and WSL against the packaged worker JAR.
- Added `tools/verify-worker-label-kill.py`: requests are sent to a live worker and its process is forcibly killed without reading the reply. A fresh process retries each producer/delivery key, verifies one pair and an empty queue after consumption, and checks that at least one producer commit and one delivery receipt had persisted before the kill. It returned `WORKER_LABEL_KILL_SUCCESS producer_commits=3 delivery_receipts=1` on both Windows and WSL. This proves small-fixture lost-acknowledgement recovery, not a kill at every write/fsync/rename boundary.
- Wired the restart, concurrency, and kill verifiers into the Linux validation and Windows package CI jobs. Workflow YAML parsed locally (`YAML_OK`); hosted CI execution is still unverified. Sustained contention, deterministic crash-point fault injection, retention/compaction, and full Zingg workflow parity remain open. Checklist remains **38/53 (71.7%)**.

### 2026-09-27 atomic snapshot failure boundaries

- The persistent provider now accepts a package-private write observer used only by tests; production construction has a no-op observer. The test injects failures after temporary-file creation, after `FileChannel.force(true)`, and after `ATOMIC_MOVE`. Before move it requires no published snapshot and no leftover `.partial` file, then a clean retry. After move it requires persisted producer/delivery receipts and exactly one pair on retry.
- Focused `PersistentLabelDecisionProviderTest` passed **11/11** on Windows and WSL; full Windows `./mvnw.cmd verify -q` passed. These are controlled exception boundaries, not actual OS process halts at the exact instructions. The worker-kill verifier separately covers lost replies but cannot force a crash inside each file operation. The larger durability/parity gate remains open at **38/53 (71.7%)**.

### 2026-09-27 child-JVM hard-stop recovery

- Added a test-only `PersistentLabelCrashProbe` child JVM that invokes `Runtime.halt(73)` at each snapshot observer stage for both producer enqueue and delivery. The parent test verifies exit code 73, unchanged/absent published snapshot before atomic move, changed snapshot after move, orphan `.partial` files before recovery, and exactly one pair after same-key retry.
- Provider construction now acquires the JVM and OS store locks, then removes only regular `.labels-*.partial` files inside that label root before loading the published snapshot. This avoids deleting a concurrent writer's temporary file and cleans up files left by a hard-stop probe.
- Focused provider tests passed **13/13** on Windows and WSL; full Windows `./mvnw.cmd verify -q` passed. This is deterministic process termination at three observer boundaries, not a power-loss test or proof of crash consistency inside an individual filesystem syscall. Sustained contention, retention, directory-sync/power-loss policy, and full Zingg parity remain open. Checklist remains **38/53 (71.7%)**.

### 2026-09-27 bounded label snapshot and negative inputs

- Snapshot serialization now uses a bounded output stream that refuses a write before crossing its 64 MiB envelope budget, instead of building an arbitrarily large intermediate payload and checking only afterward. Unit checks cover the byte boundary and verify an oversized UTF payload publishes no snapshot while a later small enqueue still succeeds.
- Focused provider tests: **16 total, 0 failures** on Windows and WSL. The symlink-rejection case was **skipped on Windows** because that runner could not create symbolic links; it ran on WSL with **0 skips**. The test confirms that symlinked `labels.bin` and `labels.lock` are rejected without altering their external target.
- Rebuilt the shaded worker (`./mvnw.cmd -pl runtime-worker -am package -q` passed). The three-process worker smoke returned `WORKER_LABEL_RESTART_SUCCESS` on Windows and WSL after adding a 10,001-unit payload rejection, no-published-snapshot check, and subsequent `ping` reuse. Full Windows `./mvnw.cmd verify -q` passed.
- Still missing: Windows symlink-capable runner evidence, permissions/read-only matrix, independent large-payload stress, receipt retention/compaction, and power-loss durability. No build-plan checkbox was closed; **38/53 (71.7%)**.

### 2026-09-27 worker command failure matrix

- Added `tools/verify-worker-failure-matrix.py` and ran it against the rebuilt worker JAR on Windows and WSL (`WORKER_FAILURE_MATRIX_SUCCESS` on both). It checks no labels, one class, `UNKNOWN` only, unmatched/duplicate IDs, missing feature/block schema, malformed training payload, corrupt classifier checksum, corrupt persistent-label snapshot, output-root traversal, and a 1-byte output cap.
- Every recoverable command failure must leave no published model/output and allow `ping` or a subsequent request; a corrupt snapshot must instead prevent worker startup without modifying the snapshot. The Windows run also passed using the locally bundled Java executable. Added the verifier to Linux validation and Windows package CI steps; hosted execution remains unverified.
- This closes only the enumerated native-fixture negative cases, not phase-by-phase cancellation/rollback, partial-directory inventories, broad adversarial input coverage, or released Zingg v0.7 differential semantics. Checklist remains **38/53 (71.7%)**.

### 2026-09-27 pinned upstream ZFrame inventory

- Added `tools/verify-zframe-inventory.py` and `docs/zframe-v07-inventory.json`. Java 21 `javap -p` enumerates **93 public abstract signatures** across **66 names** in `zingg.common.client.ZFrame<D,R,C>`; the verifier requires the normalized pinned capsule SHA-256 `edde9b14598c83e6cad2a06b9ea3e53c8e88ae7b70c3a48c6d247d73b8c737cb` and exact signature-set equality. ### 2026-09-28 ZFrame group-count NULL semantics

- Compared the adapter to `SparkFrame.java` at the pinned upstream commit [`48cb157b4f35fcfa733e2e7f9699ea988e018738`](https://github.com/zinggAI/zingg/blob/48cb157b4f35fcfa733e2e7f9699ea988e018738/spark/client/src/main/java/zingg/spark/client/SparkFrame.java). ### 2026-09-28 `withColumn` expression parity correction

- The Spark reference case for `withColumn` exposed a real defect: a profile-only `DuckZColumn` implements the public `Expression` interface, but `DuckFrame.withColumn` recognized only the concrete `DuckExpr` class. It therefore embedded the Java object as a string literal instead of evaluating its SQL expression.
- Updated `DuckFrame.withColumn` to treat any public `Expression` as an expression, pass its SQL through the read-only expression safety check, and preserve non-expression inputs as literals. Added an engine regression using a custom `Expression` (`value + 1`) and expanded the Spark differential for a string literal, a nullable boolean expression, and simultaneous two-column replacement via `withColumns`.
- Focused Windows and WSL verification passed: `ZFrameCoreDifferentialTest` **1/1** and `DuckFrameMetadataTest` **12/12** in each environment. The matrix proves literal values, expression evaluation/NULL propagation, schema, simultaneous source-column semantics, and row multiplicities.
- The first attempt failed because Scala 2.12.10 (the pinned Zingg capsule compiler runtime) overrode Spark 3.5.5's Scala 2.12.18 runtime, breaking Spark generated-lambda deserialization. The reference module now pins Spark's Scala patch version 2.12.18 while retaining the capsule's isolated 2.12.10 compile profile. The opt-in test is wired into Linux CI, but that hosted job has not yet run. This is an executable differential slice, not full interface parity; global plan remains **38/53 (71.7%)**.

### 2026-09-28 ZFrame schema rendering and unsigned types

- The Spark differential exposed that `showSchema()` returned Java `Column` record diagnostics instead of upstream Spark `StructType(...)`, and `FieldData` leaked DuckDB JDBC names such as `INTEGER`/`VARCHAR`. The opt-in adapter now renders Spark-style `StructType`/`StructField`, maps common JDBC scalar types to Spark names, and returns those names from `fields()`.
- Unsigned DuckDB integer metadata is widened to a Spark type that can preserve the full value domain (`UTINYINT→ShortType`, `USMALLINT→IntegerType`, `UINTEGER→LongType`, `UBIGINT→DecimalType(20,0)`). Other DuckDB types without a proven Spark representation remain a compatibility gap; unknown names are not silently coerced.
- The differential compares field names and types to Spark and verifies DuckDB metadata never claims stricter non-nullability than Spark. JDBC/`DESCRIBE` reports nullability as unknown/YES even for some literal-only projections that Spark marks non-nullable, so full `StructField.nullable` parity is **not** claimed. The test passed **1/1 on Windows and 1/1 on WSL** (latest WSL action time **155.6 s**, Maven exit 0); exact adapter tests passed **3/3 on Windows and WSL** with scalar schema checks.

### 2026-09-28 Native LINK collision-safe pair keys

- Audit found native LINK canonicalized an edge key by concatenating endpoint IDs with NUL. DuckDB permits embedded NUL characters in VARCHAR, so distinct pairs `("a\0b","c")` and `("a","b\0c")` produced the same composite key and could silently merge graph components.
- Replaced delimiter encoding in both edge deduplication and pair-score lookup with an ordered `PairKey(left,right)` record. Added a regression that creates the colliding IDs using `chr(0)` and requires four output entities in two distinct components.
- `ZinggPipelineTest` passed **10/10 on Windows and 10/10 on WSL**; the WSL Surefire report has 0 failures/errors/skips. This repairs the backend-native LINK profile only; approved Zingg LINK semantics/goldens remain external and the global checklist stays **38/53 (71.7%)**.

### 2026-09-28 native LINK graph phase

- Added opt-in native LINK registration for already-scored pair relations. The accepted pair columns are `z_zid` (left ID), `z_z_zid` (right ID), and `z_score`; IDs are preserved as strings, pair direction is canonicalized, duplicate/reversed pairs retain the maximum score, and self-links are ignored. Component summaries emit `z_zid,z_minScore,z_maxScore,z_cluster`; missing pair scores on transitive implied pairs contribute zero. Cluster IDs use the injected compatibility clock and the lexically smallest string ID in the component. The implementation enforces configured frame row/collect budgets plus hard native caps of one million input edges and one million output entities.
- Exact pipeline tests cover a transitive three-record component (including a duplicate reversed pair), an independent two-record component, deterministically ordered rows/cluster IDs, string IDs, empty input with retained output schema, absent required columns, NULL endpoint rejection, non-finite score rejection, configured row-budget enforcement, oversized-input rejection before collect, typed results, and run-schema cleanup. Component min/max computation uses incident-edge aggregation and a component completeness check rather than a per-entity scan of every member. Results are materialized using prepared-statement batches of 1,000 into a typed table owned by the current DuckDB run schema; failed insertions drop the partial table, and job close removes registered materializations. The low-level batch test inserts 2,501 rows across three batches, verifies BIGINT/DOUBLE schema, verifies cleanup after a malformed later row, and confirms run-schema removal. Focused LINK/ownership suites passed on Windows and WSL; latest reports: `ZinggPipelineTest` **10/10**, `DuckRuntimeOwnershipTest` **8/8** on Windows. Full Windows `./mvnw.cmd verify -q` passed after the oversized-input guard; `python -m pytest -q` passed **11/11**; `git diff --check` passed (Git emitted only the existing Windows LF→CRLF advisory warnings).
- This is a native backend LINK contract, deliberately not asserted to be Zingg v0.7's output contract. Exact LINK output names/order, ID generation, duplicate/asymmetry rules, clock semantics and scores still require approved upstream fixtures. Build-plan progress remains **38/53 (71.7%)** because the complete phase/parity checklist gate is not closed.

- Replaced fixture-specific Spark stage UIDs and Parquet UUID filename with discovery scoped to `classifier/best.model/bestModel/stages`: exactly one metadata-declared VectorAssembler, PolynomialExpansion, and LogisticRegressionModel stage must exist, and the LogisticRegression data directory must contain exactly one regular `.parquet` file. Symlinked directories/metadata/data are rejected; metadata is capped at 1 MiB per file and Parquet at 512 MiB before DuckDB opens it.

Tests require no model publication for ambiguous inputs. The existing fractional-degree regression also remains in the fixture suite.

### 2026-09-28 Arrow ingress partial-table rollback

- Reproduced an ingress cleanup gap: a valid two-row Arrow stream under a one-row job budget failed after creating/registering its job-schema table, leaving that table present until job close. `ArrowFileSupport.read` now drops the table immediately for any post-create failure and preserves cleanup errors as suppressed exceptions.
- Added `arrowIngressBudgetFailureDropsPartialJobTableImmediately`, which asserts the stable row-budget diagnostic and queries `information_schema.tables` before closing the job to prove that no table remains.
- `DuckFrameMetadataTest` passed **13/13 on Windows and WSL**; full Windows `./mvnw.cmd -q verify` passed; `python -m pytest -q` passed **11/11**; `git diff --check` found no whitespace errors (only existing Windows CRLF advisories).
- The Arrow gate remains open: vectorized ingress/performance comparison, nested-type contract, and broader cancellation/kill/cleanup qualification are not proven. Global checklist remains **37/53 (69.8%)**.

### 2026-09-28 Arrow timestamp unit and timezone preservation

- Corrected Arrow ingress, which previously interpreted every timestamp's raw integer as milliseconds and discarded schema timezone metadata. SECOND/MILLISECOND/MICROSECOND/NANOSECOND now convert with floor-safe epoch arithmetic; DuckDB's microsecond precision is applied explicitly. Timezone-free Arrow timestamps map to `TIMESTAMP` as UTC-based local date-time fields, while timezone-bearing timestamps map to `TIMESTAMPTZ` and retain the UTC instant.
- Added a two-row Arrow stream covering all four units, a non-UTC timezone declaration, post-epoch precision, and negative pre-epoch values (including -1 microsecond/nanosecond). The test confirms exact JDBC-visible timestamps and UTC instant preservation.
- `DuckFrameMetadataTest` passed **14/14 on Windows and WSL**; full Windows `./mvnw.cmd -q verify` passed; Python passed **11/11**; `git diff --check` found no whitespace errors (only existing line-ending advisories).
- Timestamp conversion coverage improves the Arrow gate but does not close it: vectorized ingress/performance, nested types, malformed later-batch/cancellation/kill behavior, and hosted platforms remain open. Current checklist remains **37/53 (69.8%)**.

### 2026-09-28 Arrow UINT64 domain preservation

- Reproduced that Arrow `Int(64, unsigned)` was mapped to DuckDB `BIGINT`, which cannot represent values above `2^63-1`; additionally, Arrow Java exposes smaller unsigned values as a mix of `Character` and signed primitive wrappers, and the generic fallback stringified some of them. UINT8/16/32 now normalize numerically into `BIGINT`; UINT64 maps to `DECIMAL(20,0)` and converts losslessly from `BigInteger` or unsigned long bits to `BigDecimal` before JDBC binding.
- Added an Arrow IPC ingress regression containing every unsigned maximum (255, 65,535, 4,294,967,295, and 18,446,744,073,709,551,615) and asserting both resulting DuckDB types and exact values.
- `DuckFrameMetadataTest` passed **15/15 on Windows and WSL**; full Windows `./mvnw.cmd -q verify` passed; Python passed **11/11**; `git diff --check` found no whitespace errors (only Git's existing line-ending advisories).
- This closes the four unsigned-width maxima in the local Arrow IPC path, not the signed integer matrix, nested Arrow contract, or ingress performance gate. Overall checklist remains **37/53 (69.8%)**.

### 2026-09-28 focused Arrow ingress baseline

It excludes one warmup, reports medians/file bytes/rows-per-second/bytes-per-second, and verifies row cardinality each run. Manual Java invocation must include the Arrow-required `java.nio` and `sun.nio.ch` module opens.

Raw environment/results: `benchmarks/arrow-ingress-2026-09-28-windows.json` and `benchmarks/arrow-ingress-2026-09-28-wsl.json`.

The measurements justify a focused vectorized C Data/CTAS experiment, but do not yet justify adding its JNI/native dependency or replacing row-wise ingress. Global checklist remains **37/53 (69.8%)**.
