### 2026-09-28 POSIX path-policy and Python test portability

- A full WSL `./mvnw -q verify` exposed that URI detection in `PathPolicy` assumed the `://` spelling survived `Path` parsing. The Linux default provider normalizes `https://host/...` to `https:/host/...`; the policy now rejects URI-like schemes followed by slash/backslash while preserving Windows drive-absolute paths. Regression tests cover HTTPS, S3, and file URI forms on POSIX and Windows drive paths on both systems.
- The same WSL run exposed a Python test hard-coding executable name `python`, unavailable in this WSL image (which provides `python3`). It now launches `sys.executable`, making the test follow the active interpreter.
- Focused `PathPolicyTest` passed on Windows and WSL. Full Windows `./mvnw.cmd -q verify` and WSL `./mvnw -q verify` both passed. Python `pytest -q` passed **11/11 on Windows and WSL** after the portability fix. WSL's first pytest attempt had one actual executable-name failure and an unrelated installed `pytest-playwright` reporting-hook internal error that masked the traceback; disabling plugin autoload isolated the test, and the normal suite passed after the code fix.
- These fixes improve path/test portability only. No build-plan gate closes; the broader checklist remains **37/53 (69.8%)**.

### 2026-09-28 Spark-compatible with-replacement sampling

- Implemented with-replacement sampling in `DuckFrame.sample`: each source row receives one materialized uniform variate; a once-materialized inverse Poisson CDF for λ=fraction (0..1, matching Spark Dataset sample bounds) determines its replication count; lateral series expansion emits that many copies. The CDF candidate range through 32 ensures coverage for λ≤1. The ZFrame adapter now forwards both replacement modes; both sample signatures are mapped, moving the exact matrix from 91 mapped/2 conditional to **93 mapped/0 conditional**. No-replacement behavior is unchanged.
- Expanded exact contract checks for both float/double overloads, zero/intermediate/unit fractions, empty input, and rejection above the supported upper bound. Expanded Spark 3.5.5 differential tests over 20,000 unique IDs at λ=0.25 for both overloads. Each engine is checked separately for total count, number of represented source IDs, number of IDs repeated more than once, and source-ID bounds; the test deliberately does not compare random row identities. Spark's API describes replacement fraction as expected multiplicity and not an exact output fraction ([Spark RDD.sample 3.5.6 API](https://spark.apache.org/docs/3.5.6/api/java/org/apache/spark/rdd/RDD.html)).
- Canonical-capsule selected reactor passed on Windows: `Zingg07ExactCompileChecks` **3/3**, `ZFrameCoreDifferentialTest` **1/1** (Surefire 24.10 s; reactor exit 0). The same tests passed on WSL2's native Linux filesystem: **3/3** and **1/1** (Surefire 24.34 s; reactor exit 0). The Linux copy avoids the previously observed severe JVM classpath lookup slowdown on `/mnt/c`; it is local WSL evidence, not hosted native-Linux evidence.
- Ran the complete default test reactor with `zingg07.contract.profile=true` on Windows and the refreshed Linux-filesystem WSL2 copy. Both completed **BUILD SUCCESS across all 10 modules**, including Spark differential and all ordinary module suites. Windows Surefire XML totals: **137 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors, 2 skipped**; the skips are the symlink-attack fixture (host privilege unavailable) and a remote-URI fixture whose Windows `Path` provider rejects the URI before `PathPolicy` receives it. WSL2's terminal output confirms every module and the reference differential succeeded. The exact `Zingg07ExactCompileChecks` (not included by Surefire's default class-name patterns) was separately run as part of the selected reactor above.
- Matrix and exhaustive semantic gates remain open; global plan remains **37/53 (69.8%)**.

### 2026-09-28 Python worker framing and payload validation

- The Python client previously bounded outgoing lines but used unbounded `stdout.readline()` for worker responses. It now performs a bounded read at the 8 MiB protocol limit, requires newline termination, and closes the subprocess on oversized/unterminated responses. Versioned payload decoding now rejects invalid counts, malformed Base64 alphabets, and invalid UTF-8 rather than silently accepting ignored characters.
- Added tests for bounded response reads and client teardown, unterminated lines, malformed frame shape, correlation mismatch, worker error status, and malformed payload encodings. Windows `unittest` and `compileall` pass (**13/13**); WSL2 `unittest` and `compileall` also pass (**13/13**). These are local platform checks, not the outstanding Python 3.10–3.14 and clean-install matrix. The overall 53-item build checklist remains **37/53 (69.8%)**; this closes only narrow protocol-hardening cases within existing open gates.
- Follow-up found the JVM worker's input cap was checked only after `BufferedReader.readLine()` had already accumulated the full request. Replaced it with a bounded line accumulator that drains excess bytes/chars through LF, handles CRLF, reports an error without trusting an undecoded request ID, and keeps the server alive. `ConfiguredWorkerServerTest` verifies exactly-at-limit input, over-limit recovery, a subsequent valid `ping`, and `shutdown`; it passed on Windows and WSL2 (`runtime-worker -am`, 1/1; WSL reactor BUILD SUCCESS). Python remained green at 13/13 on both systems. WSL ran from `/mnt/c`, so this is Linux runtime evidence with shared-filesystem build latency, not native-WSL-filesystem performance evidence. Full hosted matrix remains open; global checklist stays **37/53 (69.8%)**.
- Added a shared Python/Java wire-format golden covering backslash-plus-letters, tab, LF, CR and Unicode. `ProtocolCodecTest` passed **5/5** on Windows and WSL2; Python client tests passed **13/13** on both. This verifies encoder interoperability for the exercised vector, not the entire worker-operation payload schema or external Python-version matrix.

### 2026-09-29 strict native classifier manifest and dimension consistency

### 2026-09-29 Arrow floating precision round-trip

- Arrow IPC ingress previously mapped every `FloatingPoint` field to DuckDB `DOUBLE`; egress also emitted every JDBC floating type as Arrow `DOUBLE`/`Float8Vector`. Ingress now maps Arrow `HALF`/`SINGLE` to DuckDB `FLOAT` and `DOUBLE` to DuckDB `DOUBLE`; egress emits Arrow `SINGLE` for JDBC `FLOAT`/`REAL`, `DOUBLE` for JDBC `DOUBLE`, and writes values using `Float4Vector`/`Float8Vector`. A follow-up found that Arrow `Float2Vector` stores raw IEEE-754 half bits in a `Short`; generic numeric conversion treated those bits as an integer. Added explicit binary16-to-float decoding, including subnormal values, signed zero, infinity, and NaN. HALF necessarily widens to DuckDB FLOAT/SINGLE; value preservation is tested, but physical 16-bit type preservation is not supported by DuckDB's type system.
- `arrowHalfSingleAndDoublePrecisionSurviveIngressAppenderAndEgress` compares JDBC and Appender schemas and complete rows across HALF/SINGLE/DOUBLE, directly inspects IPC precision metadata, and re-ingests the output. FP16 cases include 1.5, minimum and maximum subnormal, minimum normal, largest finite, negative zero, positive infinity, NaN, and NULL. `DuckFrameMetadataTest` passed **18/18 on Windows and WSL2** after this follow-up. Full-reactor outcomes and freshly aggregated report counts for the latest run are recorded below once the WSL reactor completes. Broader Arrow type/failure coverage, C Data/CTAS comparison, and resource/performance qualification remain open; global build progress remains **37/53 (69.8%)**.

### 2026-09-29 atomic model publication and final integrity verification

- `ModelArtifactWriter` previously caught `AtomicMoveNotSupportedException` and retried with an ordinary move, violating the build-plan's fail-closed requirement. Its rollback handler also treated a backup-cleanup error after a successful publication as a publication failure and could delete the verified new artifact. Replaced fallback moves with atomic-only moves; publication failure now removes the new tree when needed and attempts an atomic restore. Backup cleanup failure after success emits a path-redacted warning and keeps the verified artifact plus recoverable backup instead of rolling back.
- Before reporting success, the writer now reads back the published files without following final-file symlinks and verifies exact manifest fields/features, payload SHA-256, provenance fields and provenance checksum. Verification failure rolls back to the prior artifact. This validates envelope integrity without requiring legacy serialized payloads to be interpreted as native JSON models.
- Added injected-failure tests for unsupported atomic moves, failed staged rename/restore, post-rename checksum corruption, prior-backup cleanup failure, and a symlink destination. `ModelFormatTest` passed on WSL2: **17 tests, 0 failures/errors/skips**; Windows: **17 tests, 0 failures/errors, 1 skipped** because this runner lacks symlink creation privilege. Full reactor `verify -q` passed after the writer implementation on Windows (**26 suites / 180 tests / 0 failures / 0 errors / 4 skipped**) and WSL2 (**26 / 180 / 0 / 0 / 2**); those full runs preceded the final path-redaction and symlink-test-only changes, which were then covered by the focused module tests on both platforms.
- This closes local atomic-fallback/final-checksum mechanics only; it does not prove filesystem power-loss durability, all failure interleavings, or semantic compatibility of the external artifacts. Global build-plan progress remains **37/53 (69.8%)**.

### 2026-09-29 mandatory OSV vulnerability workflow

It builds and verifies a native Linux package on push, pull request, and weekly schedule, uploads the exact package SPDX SBOM, then makes OSV scan it; findings and scanner failures block the job. Hosted execution, scanned package count, vulnerability results, and human triage remain pending; OSV SBOM/PURL support is documented by the upstream [scanner documentation](https://google.github.io/osv-scanner/scan-source/).

### 2026-09-29 no-follow label snapshot/lock opens

- Closed a path-based check/open race in `PersistentLabelDecisionProvider`: lock channels now include `NOFOLLOW_LINKS`; snapshot reads open one no-follow `FileChannel`, validate the size from its handle, and read that same handle. This also removes the separate path `size()`/`readAllBytes()` race and bounds allocation to `MAX_BYTES`.
- Added a post-construction replacement regression for both lock and snapshot files, attempting to point each at an outside sentinel. Direct no-follow opens fail and the sentinel bytes remain unchanged. `PersistentLabelDecisionProviderTest` passed **32 tests, 0 failures/errors** on Windows (2 environment skips) and **32/32** on WSL2 (no skips); both selected Maven reactors ended `BUILD SUCCESS`.
- This mitigates final-component symlink substitution; it does not claim protection against every parent-directory replacement race, filesystem/device corruption, attacker-controlled directory ancestors, sustained hostile concurrent mutation, or power loss. Full worker/default-policy and hosted-platform evidence remain open. Global checklist remains **37/53 (69.8%)**.

### 2026-09-29 parent-directory race capability check

- Probed Java NIO directory-stream capabilities on the current Windows filesystem and WSL2 Linux filesystem: Windows returned `WindowsDirectoryStream` (`SecureDirectoryStream=false`); WSL2 returned `UnixSecureDirectoryStream` (`true`). This confirms the secure directory-relative API is not uniformly available on the required platforms.
- The current snapshot writer uses path-based temporary creation and `Files.move(..., ATOMIC_MOVE, REPLACE_EXISTING)`. Java's portable `SecureDirectoryStream` API has no replace-existing option for `move`, so switching only Linux to descriptor-relative publication would not provide a matching Windows implementation or a uniform guarantee.
- Decision: do not claim the parent-directory substitution race is closed. Keep the existing final-component `NOFOLLOW_LINKS` defenses and record this as a residual threat-model limitation until an OS-specific, audited native implementation (or a storage design that removes attacker-writable parent directories) is selected. This is a capability/design check, not a code fix or a passing security test. Global checklist remains **37/53 (69.8%)**.

### 2026-09-29 POSIX label-store file permissions

- Newly created POSIX `labels.lock` and staged snapshot files are now created with explicit owner-only read/write mode (`0600`), independent of a permissive process umask; the published snapshot retains that mode through the same-directory atomic rename. Providers on filesystems without POSIX attribute support retain platform defaults.
- Added a filesystem-backed regression that creates and populates the store, then checks exact permissions of both persistent files. `PersistentLabelDecisionProviderTest` passed **33 tests, 0 failures/errors** on Windows (3 environment skips because POSIX permissions are unavailable) and **33/33 on WSL2** (the exact mode assertion runs). Windows ACLs remain inherited from the containing directory and were not hardened by this change.
- This reduces disclosure risk for newly created POSIX stores but does not repair permissions on pre-existing files, secure ancestor directories, close parent-directory replacement races, or establish Windows ACL policy. Focused reactors ended `BUILD SUCCESS`; global checklist remains **37/53 (69.8%)** because the containing end-to-end persistence gate still has unmet acceptance criteria.

### 2026-09-29 fail-closed handling of permissive existing label files

- Lock and snapshot channels now verify POSIX permissions are exactly owner read/write (`0600`) after opening with `NOFOLLOW_LINKS`. If an existing file is group/other accessible, the channel is closed and the provider rejects the operation rather than silently reading/writing sensitive state. It does not attempt path-based chmod because that would add a symlink-following mutation risk; an operator must repair permissions before reopening.
- Added regressions that loosen each file to `0640`, require rejection, restore `0600`, and prove access resumes. Updated malformed-snapshot fixture writers to enforce private modes so permission checks cannot mask parser assertions. `PersistentLabelDecisionProviderTest` passed **34 tests, 0 failures/errors** on Windows (4 POSIX-only environment skips) and **34/34 on WSL2**; both focused Maven reactors ended `BUILD SUCCESS`.
- On Windows, POSIX attributes are unavailable and inherited ACLs remain the effective policy; no Windows ACL inspection/restriction has been implemented. POSIX attribute lookup is still path-based rather than tied to the opened file descriptor, so this does not close a hostile concurrent parent/path replacement race. Global checklist remains **37/53 (69.8%)**.

### 2026-09-29 isolated blocking-importer output path preflight

- Fixed the legacy importer process launcher to reject output paths equal to or nested under the input using both normalized and `toRealPath()` input roots, and to reject symlink components in the output path before creating directories or starting the child process. Added regressions for nested output and a symlinked output pointing at an external sentinel.
- The nested-path regression exposed a Windows-specific alias mismatch: the temporary input's normalized path used an 8.3 `ANGEL~1` component while `toRealPath()` returned the long user directory, so comparing only the real path let the child-launch path execute. ### 2026-09-29 Arrow single-handle no-follow ingestion

- `ArrowFileSupport.openReader` previously inspected IPC magic through one path-based input stream, then reopened the path for Arrow parsing; the Arrow file branch also opened a separate channel without `NOFOLLOW_LINKS`. This admitted a final-component symlink/check-open race and could parse a different file from the one probed.
- It now opens a single `FileChannel` with `READ` and `NOFOLLOW_LINKS`, performs positional magic reads, and transfers that same channel to either `ArrowFileReader` or a channel-backed `ArrowStreamReader`. The channel is closed on every setup failure and owned by the reader after successful construction.
- Added a symlink-to-external-sentinel regression requiring open rejection, no `zd_arrow_*` table, and unchanged sentinel bytes. `DuckFrameMetadataTest` passed **21/21 on WSL2** (symlink test executed) and **21 tests, 0 failures, 1 symlink-capability skip on Windows**; both focused Maven dependency builds ended `BUILD SUCCESS`.
- This removes a final-file check/open/reopen race; it does not prevent hostile replacement of parent directories or prove decode-originated fault, cancellation, or worker-kill cleanup. Arrow performance/resource and full compatibility gate remains open; global raw checklist stays **37/53 (69.8%)**.

### 2026-09-29 Arrow interruption cleanup

- `ArrowFileSupport` removed partially imported materialization state when the batch observer raised `InterruptedException`, but the broad exception wrapper did not restore the Java thread's interrupt flag. The cleanup now re-interrupts the thread before dropping the partial table and wrapping the failure.
- Added a two-batch regression for both JDBC and Appender paths. It injects interruption after the first committed batch, checks the wrapped cause/message, checks that the thread remains interrupted, and verifies that the job schema and materialization registry contain no partial table. The test clears the interrupt flag in `finally` to avoid contaminating the test runner.
- `DuckFrameMetadataTest` passed **22/22 on WSL2** and **22 tests, 0 failures, 1 Windows symlink-capability skip**; focused dependency reactors ended `BUILD SUCCESS` on both.
- This tests the deterministic interruption boundary through the package-private batch observer; it is not proof that JDBC/Appender IO responds to a real worker cancellation or process kill. Arrow release gate remains open; checklist remains **37/53 (69.8%)**.

### 2026-09-29 Python worker process cancellation

- The Java configured server reads and executes requests serially, so a `cancel` protocol line cannot be consumed during a long-running operation; the existing `DuckCancellation` is not wired from worker request IDs into pipeline statements. Per-request cooperative Java/JDBC cancellation remains unimplemented.
- Added explicit process-wide `WorkerClient.cancel()` and `DuckWorker.cancel()`: terminate the subprocess, wait five seconds, escalate to kill on timeout, and wait for exit. `close()` reuses this termination path and then closes pipes. Documented that cancellation is whole-process, requires a new worker for subsequent requests, and does not guarantee rollback of already committed side effects.
- Added an integration test that starts a real blocked child process, confirms it consumed the request, cancels it from the test thread, and verifies the active `readline()` unblocks on EOF; a second test verifies terminate→kill escalation. Python client suite passed **15 tests, 0 failures, 7 subtests** on Windows and WSL2 with warnings treated as errors.
- This verifies subprocess cancellation, not request-selective SQL cancellation, Java server reuse, or transaction rollback. Those stay explicit work items; global checklist remains **37/53 (69.8%)**.
- Full Python validation after the API change: Windows repository-wide pytest with `PYTHONWARNINGS=error` passed **94 tests, 10 skips, 48 subtests**; packaging unittest discovery passed **89 tests, 10 skips**. WSL2 passed pytest **103 tests, 1 skip, 48 subtests** and packaging discovery **89 tests, 1 skip**. Skips are platform/tool capability cases. The two runtimes use the same Windows-mounted working tree; these results do not establish native Linux filesystem behavior or hosted CI.

### 2026-09-29 Arrow JDBC/Appender write error after prior commit

- Added a second two-batch IPC fault fixture: batch one contains an in-range `DECIMAL(5,2)` and commits; batch two retains valid IPC framing but contains a raw 128-bit unscaled decimal payload beyond the declared precision. Arrow's public vector setter correctly refuses this malformed semantic value, so the test writes the underlying vector bytes to reach the backend write boundary. The test asserts the wrapped cause is `java.sql.SQLException`, proving the error comes from DuckDB's JDBC/Appender write path rather than Arrow decoding, then verifies table and materialization cleanup.
- `DuckFrameMetadataTest` passed **24/24** on Ubuntu/WSL native Linux filesystem and **24 tests, 0 failures, 1 symlink-capability skip** on Windows. Full `engine-api` + `engine-duckdb` dependency reactors passed **63/63** on Ubuntu and **63 tests, 0 failures, 1 skip** on Windows; each ended `BUILD SUCCESS`.
- This is deliberate invalid-input fault injection, not a claim that valid Arrow producers emit out-of-precision decimals. Real child-process termination during Arrow import, request-selective cancellation, RSS/spill profiling, and independent-runner qualification remain unproven; broader Arrow gate and checklist remain **37/53 (69.8%)**.

The crash regression now verifies both orphan table and schema removal after reopen.

### 2026-09-30 pipeline-output cancellation commit boundary

- `ZinggPipeline.execute()` now checks the request token before execution, before output generation/publication, and immediately before atomic mover entry. Output row count is captured before publication so a cancellation arriving after the atomic rename cannot turn a successfully committed result into an error response. The documented race rule is explicit: cancellation observed before mover entry aborts and preserves the old destination; once atomic mover entry begins, a completed atomic rename wins and the pipeline reports committed success.
- Added deterministic regressions for both sides of that boundary: a pre-cancelled pipeline preserves an existing destination and leaves no partial; an injected atomic mover cancels during commit and still atomically publishes the new result, with a successful pipeline result and no temp artifact. Focused `ZinggPipelineTest` passed **18/18 on Windows and 18/18 on WSL**, with both reactor builds successful.
- Rebuilt a fresh Linux distribution from the updated source into `dist-docker-output-publish-20260930` using `CREATE_JRE=true`; all nine Maven modules and package/SBOM/SPDX/notices/JDK-legal/inventory/rollback validations succeeded. The follow-up networkless, read-only Docker verifier passed Ubuntu 24.04 launcher smoke and **4/4** Python packaged-worker cancellation/process tests in Python 3.12-slim, including native classifier TRAIN cancellation. It emitted `DOCKER_PACKAGE_SMOKE_SUCCESS`; Docker image IDs were Ubuntu `sha256:008173c23f95b170204355c12626cb5a965d779a7e1283b09e9cffbb1bf33ca3` and Python `sha256:f77ac9e44ae96ef2c90b8053ea08c31f8be030f824196b0ae4db6d462c84e51f`.
- This does not make the entire cancellation gate complete: broader output process-kill/crash recovery, remaining mutation/cleanup failures, Future lifecycle stress, and external/upstream parity and release gates remain open. Checklist accounting remains **37/53 checked (69.8% as a raw checkbox ratio only)**; it is not a defensible whole-project completion percentage.

### 2026-09-30 pipeline output crash/retry and same-destination publication

- Closed a concrete output-file crash gap. `ZinggPipeline` now takes a JVM-local fixed 128-stripe lock and an OS file lock on a retained sibling `.<filename>.lock` for each normalized target. Under the lock it removes only direct-child regular files matching that target's reserved `.<filename>.partial-*` prefix, then serializes and atomically replaces the destination. The OS lock file is intentionally not unlinked, avoiding separate inode lock domains; symbolic lock targets fail closed. Filesystems that cannot provide atomic rename still fail closed. Locking is cooperative and only coordinates writers using this implementation.
- Added `PipelineOutputCrashProbe`, a real child JVM that calls `Runtime.halt(73)` from the atomic-mover boundary after staging is fully written and before rename. The parent verifies the old destination remains byte-identical and a staging file remains; a fresh pipeline retry cleans that orphan and publishes the complete result. Added `PipelineOutputLockProbe` and marker-coordinated independent JVMs: process one pauses inside its mover, process two confirms it has reached lock acquisition but cannot enter its mover, then both are released in sequence and leave one complete output with no staging debris. Added a same-JVM test with a real directory and symlink alias to that directory: two independent runtimes targeting the same file must serialize without `OverlappingFileLockException`. A barrier test holds publication for one `matches.csv` while an identical basename in a second directory completes, demonstrating per-canonical-destination concurrency. The JVM lock registry reference-counts waiters/holders and removes idle keys. A directory at the reserved lock-sidecar path causes actual `FileChannel.open` failure: the old output remains byte-identical, no staging file is created, and retry after removing the obstruction succeeds. An injected `AtomicMoveNotSupportedException` also preserves the old destination and removes the staging file; the same runtime then retries successfully, proving both locks are released. Symlink regressions continue to prove lock sidecars fail closed and orphan cleanup does not follow/delete links. `ZinggPipelineTest` passes **25 tests with no failures on Windows** (3 symlink-capability skips) and **25/25 on WSL** (no skips).
- Revalidated together with persistent-label recovery before adding the new two-process case: those focused suites passed **55 tests, 0 failures/errors, 5 Windows capability skips** and **55/55 on WSL with no skips**. Latest pipeline run adds deterministic lock-sidecar open failure/retry and identical-basename parallel publication across distinct directories; the suite passes **25 tests, 0 failures/errors, 3 Windows symlink-capability skips** and **25/25 on WSL**, with both five-module reactors ending `BUILD SUCCESS`. This is local process-crash/retry and destination-lock evidence, not failure injection inside the OS `FileLock.lock()` provider, power-loss or directory-entry durability, hostile non-cooperating-writer protection, network-filesystem lock qualification, or the full output crash matrix. WSL execution used the `/mnt/c` checkout (NTFS-backed), not native ext4. Overall checklist remains **37/53 (69.8% raw checkboxes only)**.
- Broader regression rerun after this output-lock change: `./mvnw.cmd -B -ntp -pl compat-zingg07-runtime -am test` passed the complete five-module reactor on Windows; compat runtime **111 tests, 0 failures/errors, 8 skips**, engine-duckdb **59 tests, 0 failures/errors, 1 skip**, and model-format **19 tests, 0 failures/errors, 2 skips**. The matching WSL reactor also ended `BUILD SUCCESS`; compat runtime **111 tests, 0 failures/errors, 2 opt-in skips**, engine-duckdb **59/59**, model-format **19/19**, and engine-api **8/8**. Thus all applicable tests in the affected reactor pass on both systems; WSL's checkout remains `/mnt/c` (NTFS-backed), not native ext4.

### 2026-09-30 `gt(String, double)` overload differential

### 2026-09-30 code-audit remediation (2026-09-24 report)

- Reconciled all 12 `ZD-*` findings against the current checkout; the audit's upstream Zingg/Spark Connect/GraphFrames/SecondString findings are explicitly out of this repository's code scope. The detailed disposition and remaining exit evidence are in `build-plan.md` under “Audit reconciliation”.
- Fixed `DuckFrame.explode`: DuckDB's `UNNEST` output is explicitly named and changed-schema metadata is refreshed. New regressions assert `columns()`, `schema()`, actual values, and subsequent selection. Added schema/composability checks for grouped aggregation, duplicate-name joins, and unaliased `selectExpr` output discovery.
- Fixed `DuckRuntime.openJob` failure ownership: if schema/job construction fails after JDBC duplication, the active-schema marker is removed, the duplicate connection is closed with cleanup failures suppressed, and the semaphore permit is returned even for unchecked failures. A read-only DuckDB database now forces schema-creation failure twice; both attempts fail promptly, proving the single job permit is returned.
- Enforced DuckDB-side offline defaults at instance startup: external access and extension autoload/autoinstall are disabled; community extensions are disabled; offline settings retain local-file access through DuckDB's allowed filesystem root. Explicit online mode only installs/loads configured allowlist entries. The instance configuration is locked after initialization and effective settings are verified after connection so JDBC URL options cannot override policy. A pinned-JDBC regression checks the settings, confirms an invalid HTTPS CSV cannot trigger implicit connector loading, confirms allowed local CSV reading remains functional, rejects a later attempt to re-enable extension loading, and rejects a URL that requests `enable_external_access=true` in offline mode. This does not replace configured worker path roots or prove all external-access vectors on every OS.
- Removed clique expansion from `GraphOutput.entityScores`; it calculates connectivity and zero-aware min/max from sparse direct-edge maps. Added a 10,000-vertex chain regression. The separate compatibility API `transitiveEdges` intentionally materializes implied pairs; it now pre-counts output and enforces a 1,000,000-pair default budget, with explicit budget/cancellation overloads. Product approval and capacity benchmarks remain open.
- Reworked experimental native classifier fitting to snapshot input once in DuckDB and compute full-batch gradients via SQL aggregates; Java receives only the gradient vector per iteration instead of retaining up to one million boxed rows, and every iteration sees the same materialized rows. Added a one-step exact-gradient regression. Cancellation testing exposed and fixed loss of cancellation identity when DuckDB returned a cancelled JDBC error during `collect`.
- Hardened Python worker client lifecycle: stderr is inherited rather than left in an unread pipe; `request_timeout` defaults to 300 seconds, terminates a non-responsive child, and can be set to `None` for unlimited runs. Added a silent-worker timeout regression and changed process synchronization tests to use readiness markers.
- Corrected Jaccard token boundaries to the pinned SecondString default (letter runs and digit runs are separate; whitespace/punctuation delimit tokens), including its default-locale case folding. The project continues to define null/blank comparisons as score 1 instead of propagating SecondString's empty-set `NaN`; that deliberate compatibility deviation still needs explicit approval and full differential fixtures.
- Focused Maven regressions passed on Windows for `GraphOutputTest`, `DuckFrameMetadataTest`, `DuckRuntimeOwnershipTest`, `SimilarityRegistryTest`, `MatcherPairShapeTest`, and `NativeClassifierTrainerTest`. Full `mvnw verify -q` passed on Windows and full `./mvnw verify -q` passed in WSL; after the final SQL-snapshot trainer adjustment, its focused trainer/matcher tests passed on both Windows and WSL, and the latest runtime-ownership regression passed on Windows. Python tests passed **22/22** on Windows. A fresh WSL Linux/JRE package passed source archive, bundle inventory, SPDX, notices/licenses, JRE legality, rollback creation/verification, and final package verification. The newest bundle, including the SQL-snapshot trainer adjustment, passed `verify-docker-package.sh` with no network and no system Java; packaged Python/worker process tests passed **4/4** in `python:3.12-slim`, with Ubuntu 24.04 launcher ping/shutdown succeeding. Hosted GitHub Actions and macOS/ARM release runners remain unverified.
- Still open from the audit: hard peak-disk output quotas (ZD-008), graph-pair cap approval and resource qualification (ZD-003), exact upstream similarity differential/blank semantics (ZD-007), true imported-model end-to-end score parity for ZD-010/012, and hosted clean-platform CI evidence (ZD-009). No whole-project completion percentage is inferred from this audit subset.

## Output byte-guard follow-up (Docker-only)

- Added best-effort early cancellation when staged DuckDB `COPY` or Arrow IPC output crosses the configured byte budget, and wired the compatibility pipeline's output limit into the job-level resource budget. Existing post-write size enforcement remains as a backstop.
- Docker focused tests: `ResourceBudgetTest` **6/6** and `ZinggPipelineTest` **26/26** passed.
- Clean Docker `mvn -B verify`: all **9 modules** successful; engine module **76 tests**, compatibility module **119 tests / 2 skipped** (opt-in upstream probes); no failures or errors. Docker Spark reference tests: **48/48 + 1/1**.
- Fresh Linux/JRE package built and verified in Docker; offline launcher smoke passed and packaged worker/Python integration passed **4/4**. Package is in `dist-docker-output-cap-20261001/zingg-duckdb-0.1.0/zingg-duckdb-0.1.0/`.
- Limitation: this polling guard cannot guarantee a hard peak-disk cap or zero overshoot and does not bound DuckDB spill or every artifact route. P1.1 remains open pending an enforceable filesystem/container quota or bounded writer and deterministic spill/disk-full/finalization tests. No validation was run outside Docker for this follow-up.

## Worker EOF/drain lifecycle follow-up (Docker-only, 2026-10-01)

- Added `ConfiguredWorkerServerTest.eofDrainsAcceptedRequestsAndTerminatesServer`: EOF without a shutdown message drains three already accepted requests, preserves each response correlation ID, and terminates the server.
- Clean Docker reactor command `mvn -B -pl runtime-worker -am -Dtest=ConfiguredWorkerServerTest -Dsurefire.failIfNoSpecifiedTests=false test`: **6 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors, 0 skipped** across 7 selected modules.
- This is local Linux-container EOF/drain evidence only. Worker timeout escalation/reaping, startup/readiness, stderr, process-tree termination, durable-boundary hard-stop recovery, and Windows/hosted platform qualification remain open under P1.6.
- Overall checklist stays **7/86 (8.1%)**; no broad lifecycle gate was closed.
- Against the fresh Linux/JRE package in a `python:3.12-slim` container with networking disabled, a real long-running DuckDB count exceeded a configured 0.1-second `WorkerClient` timeout. The client reported worker termination, the child was reaped within 5 seconds, and a newly launched packaged worker returned `pong` (`PACKAGED_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_REAP_AND_RESTART_SUCCESS`). This covers packaged Linux/Docker timeout/reap/restart only; Windows/WSL and hard-stop recovery remain unverified.

## Arrow unsupported-schema follow-up (Docker-only, 2026-10-01)

- `DuckFrameMetadataTest.unsupportedArrowTimeAndNestedTypesFailBeforeCreatingWorkTables` verifies explicit rejection of Arrow `TIME` and nested `LIST<INT32>` fields in both JDBC and Appender paths, before staging-table creation/registration.
- Clean Docker command `mvn -B -pl engine-duckdb -am -Dtest=DuckFrameMetadataTest -Dsurefire.failIfNoSpecifiedTests=false test`: **30 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors, 0 skipped**.
- `docs/worker-protocol.md` now records the concrete scalar type mapping and timestamp precision/normalization caveats, and identifies unqualified type cases.
- P1.7 remains open; dictionary encoding, full timestamp/timezone boundaries, and complete type/cross-path qualification have not been closed. Checklist remains **7/86 (8.1%)**.

### 2026-10-01 Arrow empty IPC inputs (Docker-only)

- Expanded the empty-input regression to distinguish schema-only Arrow stream/file inputs from stream/file inputs containing one explicit zero-row record batch. Both JDBC and Appender routes must preserve the `value` schema and import zero rows.
- Focused test passed **1/1**; full `DuckFrameMetadataTest` passed **31/31**, zero failures/errors/skips, in `maven:3.9.9-eclipse-temurin-21` using a clean source copy.
- The protocol documentation now records these verified semantics. This does not close P1.7: dictionary encoding, remaining timestamp/timezone boundaries, broad type/error/resource/performance coverage, and cross-platform qualification remain pending. Progress remains **7/86 (8.1%)**.

### 2026-10-01 row-budget boundary and early cancellation (Docker-only)

- Added direct-engine coverage proving `count()` and `collect()` accept exactly the configured three-row limit and reject the first excess row without returning a truncated result. `collect()` now cancels its JDBC statement on overflow and preserves the budget exception if cancellation itself fails. A 100,000-row source with a three-row budget is rejected on row four within a 10-second bound. The worker protocol regression proves exact-boundary count success, structured excess error, worker reuse, and clean shutdown.
- In `maven:3.9.9-eclipse-temurin-21`, `ResourceBudgetTest` passed **7/7** and `ConfiguredWorkerServerTest` passed **7/7**, with zero failures/errors/skips.
- The targeted P2.3 `maxRows` boundary/early-collect-cancellation item is closed. P2.3 remains open for enforcing and validating row/collect limits on every worker ingestion and result route. The billion-row test idea was discarded after DuckDB did not yield its first row within the test's 10-second bound, before application-level row accounting.
- Checklist progress is **8/86 (9.3%)**; this task-count ratio is not a project-completion estimate.

### 2026-10-01 configured-worker Arrow row-budget rollback (Docker-only)

- Added a `run_v2` worker-protocol test that imports a four-row Arrow stream in two batches with `maxRows=3`. Rejection on batch two returns the exact `row budget exceeded: 4 > 3` diagnostic, removes the partially imported `zd_arrow_*` table, publishes neither output nor staging file, and leaves the worker able to answer `ping` and shut down.
- The clean-copy seven-module Docker reactor for `ConfiguredWorkerServerTest` passed **8/8**, zero failures/errors/skips.
- This is evidence for one real worker ingestion route, not all worker result/ingestion paths; complete row/collect-budget propagation remains open. Checklist remains **8/86 (9.3%)**.

### 2026-10-01 run_v2 Arrow row-budget rollback (Docker-only)

- Added an end-to-end worker request with a four-row/two-batch Arrow stream and a configured three-row budget. The worker rejects batch two with `row budget exceeded: 4 > 3`, leaves no partial Arrow table or output/staging file, and remains usable for ping and shutdown.
- `ConfiguredWorkerServerTest` passed **8/8** in the clean-copy seven-module Docker reactor. This covers one worker ingress route only; full route inventory and collect-byte propagation remain open. Checklist remains **8/86 (9.3%)**.

### 2026-10-01 joined-frame duplicate column metadata (Docker-only)

- A strengthened regression exposed that `DuckFrame.join()` reported duplicate input names (`group_name`, `group_name`) although DuckDB's query result named the right column `group_name_1`. The stale name prevented selecting that result column through the Frame API.
- `join()` and `joinUsing()` now derive result names from query metadata. Tests cover automatic duplicate-name suffixes, exact schema/columns agreement, selecting the generated name, and preserving its value for duplicate non-key columns in a USING join.
- Focused test passed **1/1**; complete `DuckFrameMetadataTest` passed **31/31** in Docker, zero failures/errors/skips. Broader alias, zero-column join, overload, and schema invalidation tests remain open; checklist stays **8/86 (9.3%)**.
- Follow-up clean-copy Docker reactor `mvn -B -ntp -pl compat-zingg07-runtime -am test` passed all five modules: engine-api **8/8**, model-format **19/19**, engine-duckdb **81/81**, compatibility **119 tests with 0 failures/errors and 2 opt-in probes skipped** (117 executed successfully). `BUILD SUCCESS`; broader P2.3 matrix and external probes remain open. Checklist stays **8/86 (9.3%)**.
- A follow-up duplicate-projection assertion found and fixed the same stale-name issue in `select("group_name", "group_name")`: the second output is actually named `group_name_1` by DuckDB. `select()` now exposes SQL metadata names; exact-name selection/row access is tested. The final source state passed the focused regression and the full engine/compatibility reactor (81 engine tests and 119 compatibility tests, 2 external probes skipped). Broader P2.3 schema cases remain open; checklist stays **8/86 (9.3%)**.
- Added case-insensitive identifier regressions: `withColumn("VALUE", ...)` replaces existing `value`, `rename("VALUE", "RenamedValue")` resolves the source, case-only rename works, and renaming onto a different existing column's case variant is rejected. `withColumn()`/`rename()` now derive actual output names from DuckDB metadata. The targeted test passed **1/1**, and the full five-module engine/compatibility reactor passed on this source state (engine-api 8/8; model-format 19/19; engine-duckdb 81/81; compatibility 119 tests, 2 opt-in probes skipped). Broader P2.3 matrix stays open; checklist remains **8/86 (9.3%)**.
- Extended duplicate-name metadata coverage through cache, positional union, and `joinProjected()`. The targeted regression and complete `DuckFrameMetadataTest` passed **31/31** in Docker; schema agreement, cached rows, union cardinality, projected names and right-side value are asserted. Broader P2.3 matrix remains open; checklist stays **8/86 (9.3%)**.
- Added zero-column join schema/collect assertions (sentinel stays hidden, 3×3 multiplicity preserved), zero/nonzero projected-join checks, and a case-insensitive `joinProjected()` alias-collision regression. Docker reproduced acceptance of `r_id` colliding with right alias `R_id`; collision detection now uses case-insensitive identifier comparison. Focused cases passed **2/2**, and full engine/compatibility reactor passed: engine-api **8/8**, model-format **19/19**, engine-duckdb **81/81**, compatibility **119 tests (117 pass, 2 opt-in external probes skipped)**. Overall P2.3 remains open; checklist stays **8/86 (9.3%)**.

Broader P2.3 matrix remains open; checklist stays **8/86 (9.3%)**.
- Output hardening follow-up: exercised configured output limits for CSV, Parquet, JSON, and Arrow, asserting prior destination preservation and staging cleanup; all four formats now fail closed if atomic publication is unavailable (no non-atomic fallback). `ResourceBudgetTest` passed **9/9** in Docker; five-module reactor passed engine-api **8/8**, model-format **19/19**, engine-duckdb **83/83**, compatibility **119 / 0 failures / 2 opt-in external probes skipped**. - Added a real configured-worker `run_v2` output-budget rejection test: existing destination preserved, pipeline staging removed, correlated error returned, and worker remains usable for ping/shutdown. - Docker spill-limit probe against DuckDB with `max_temp_directory_size=1 MiB`, 64 MiB memory, and a 20-million-row hash sort completed successfully while a 1 ms observer measured **532,938,752 bytes (~508 MiB)** in the configured temp directory; DuckDB diagnostics still reported `1.0 MiB`, and files were cleaned after job close. A larger workload falsifies use of this DuckDB setting alone as a hard observed directory cap for this case. The observer may miss the true peak; this is a diagnostic, not a passing quota test. A speculative enforcement assertion failed and was removed rather than leaving the suite red. A bounded temp filesystem/container mount (or equivalent) and disk-full/cancellation testing remain necessary; P1.1 and checklist stay open at **8/86 (9.3%)**.
- Added Java `--temp-directory` and Python `temp_directory` support so operators can point the worker at a quota-backed spill mount. Seven-module Docker `package` reactor passed: engine-api **8/8**, model-format **19/19**, engine-duckdb **83/83**, protocol **5/5**, compatibility **119 / 117 passed / 2 opt-in skipped**, runtime-worker **10/10**. This verifies wiring only—not exhaustion behavior or host-native quota guarantees. P1.1 remains open; checklist remains **8/86 (9.3%)**.

Probe passed and emitted `SPILL_TMPFS_QUOTA_REJECTED_AND_CLEANED`. This isolates the container filesystem cap from the DuckDB max-temp setting for this workload. The final probe was rebuilt and executed in Docker; Python forwarding and workflow tests passed **2/2**, and `sh -n`/`git diff --check` passed. Hard-kill cleanup, concurrent aggregate consumption and host-native Windows/Linux quota semantics remain unverified; checklist stays **8/86 (9.3%)**.

- With finite `maxInputBytes`, `DuckPipeReader` now copies all inputs into a private directory under DuckDB's configured temporary directory, enforces the aggregate budget during copying, reads only the copies, and removes the directory after materialization or failure. Docker regressions cover exact aggregate boundary, successful import cleanup, malformed-input cleanup, and mutation detected between preflight and copy. This stabilizes the bytes DuckDB reads after copying, but cannot exclude hostile change-and-restore during the copy itself (potentially producing mixed-version snapshot bytes). Unlimited mode (`maxInputBytes=0`) still reads source files directly with metadata change detection. - Configured worker filesystem roots: `ConfiguredWorkerServerTest.runV2EnforcesConfiguredInputAndOutputRootsAcrossWorkerProtocol` rejects an external input, output traversal, symlink input, and symlink output through correlated `run_v2` errors; it also confirms an allowed input/output succeeds, the outside symlink target remains unchanged, and the worker stays responsive for ping/shutdown. - P1.5 configured filesystem and runtime policy slice: the combined Docker Maven reactor passed `PathPolicyTest` **4/4**, `DuckRuntimeOwnershipTest` **12/12**, and `ConfiguredWorkerServerTest` **13/13**. - Similarity locale/blank characterization (Docker, 2026-10-02): added an explicit English/Turkish Jaccard matrix for dotted/dotless I, combining-dot tokenization, and punctuation/letter-digit boundaries, plus null/whitespace safe-result cases. `SimilarityRegistryTest` and `HashRegistryTest` passed **14/14**. This is local implementation behavior evidence, not comparison against the exact pinned SecondString/Zingg artifact or approval of the deliberate blank/null deviation; P1.3 parity requirements remain open.
- Arrow resource/termination verification refresh (Docker, 2026-10-02): five focused `DuckFrameMetadataTest` cases passed **5/5**, covering forced child-JVM termination after the first imported batch and restart cleanup, aggregate input-byte preflight, input mutation after preflight, row-budget rollback for JDBC/Appender, and later-batch rollback. The direct collect-byte-budget regression passed **1/1** separately. This verifies cleanup and selected limits, but does not prove collect stops before decoding/materializing an over-budget JDBC value or qualify every byte/commit boundary; P1.7/P2.3 aggregate resource work remains open.
- `DuckFrame.collect()` byte/row budget refinement (Docker, 2026-10-02): row count is checked before fetching the row's columns; UTF-8 bytes are accounted value-by-value, and each value/row is rejected before being retained in the collected result when it crosses budget. Rejection cancels the active statement. The new UTF-8 inclusive-boundary test confirms `é` fits an 11-byte budget (2 payload + 1 cell + 8 row overhead), `éa` fails at 12 bytes, and a subsequent query on the same job succeeds. Full `DuckFrameMetadataTest` passed **55/55** and `ResourceBudgetTest` **10/10**. JDBC must still materialize an individual value before its byte size can be measured, so this is not a hard heap limit; type-estimation, remaining ingestion/worker routes, and batch/commit fault coverage remain open.
- Pipeline cancellation/publication verification (Docker, 2026-10-02): all **26/26** `ZinggPipelineTest` cases passed. This includes pre-output cancellation, atomic-rename-wins cancellation semantics, output-budget destination preservation, hard child-JVM stop before output rename with subsequent orphan cleanup/retry, and MATCH/LINK pipeline coverage. It does not cover Python client's packaged-worker timeout/escalation/reaping matrix or all durable-label/Arrow cancellation boundaries; P1.6 remains open.
- Fresh packaged-worker timeout/reap/restart test (network-disabled Docker, 2026-10-02): built the current shaded worker JAR in Docker, then mounted it with the bundled Java 21 runtime and Python facade into a read-only-root `python:3.12-slim` container. `PackagedWorkerCancellationTest.test_request_timeout_reaps_packaged_worker_and_fresh_worker_recovers` timed out a real long-running DuckDB count at 50 ms, confirmed the child had exited and was synchronously reaped, and launched a new worker that returned `pong`. Full packaged process suite passed **5/5**; `python/tests/test_client.py` passed **20/20**, including terminate-to-kill escalation unit coverage. This verifies Linux-container behavior only; no Windows/hosted run or real-worker forced-escalation/process-tree test, so P1.6 remains open.
- Empty-result Arrow egress round-trip (Docker, 2026-10-02): zero-row query output retains its `label`/`identifier` schema in the IPC stream, produces no rows, and reimports to identical DuckDB schemas with empty results through JDBC and Appender; no staging files remain. Focused regression passed **1/1** and the current complete `DuckFrameMetadataTest` passed **56/56**. This closes only the empty-result egress case; P1.7's remaining vector/encoding/resource matrix is still open.
- Docker-only collection-budget follow-up (2026-10-02): `DuckFrame.collect()` now counts binary `byte[]`/`Blob` payload size rather than Java object identity text; JDBC `Blob` handles are freed after size inspection. Focused UTF-8 and binary exact-boundary/over-budget regressions passed **2/2**, including NULL handling and job reuse after rejection. The Arrow child-process readiness timeout was increased from 20 to 60 seconds after the isolated probe passed in 34.19 seconds. The isolated hard-kill/restart case passed **1/1**, then the complete `DuckFrameMetadataTest` passed **57/57** (42.89 seconds, zero failures/errors) in Docker. Earlier failed exploratory runs are superseded by this verified run. `maxCollectBytes` remains an estimate/retained-result guard, not a strict heap cap or pre-JDBC-materialization limit.

- Python worker timeout contract docs (Docker, 2026-10-02): `docs/worker-protocol.md` now explicitly records the 300-second default, `None` for no deadline, positive custom timeout, `TimeoutError` text with request ID, process termination/non-reusability, possible durable side effects, and idempotent/recovery-based retry guidance. `WorkerClientHelpersTest.test_request_timeout_terminates_silent_worker` passed **1/1**, confirming the documented diagnostic; the plan's documentation subtask is complete.
- Complex collect-budget accounting (Docker, 2026-10-02): when `maxCollectBytes` is active, `DuckFrame.collect()` no longer estimates arbitrary JDBC objects by their potentially misleading `String.valueOf()` form. Strings, byte arrays/Blob, numeric/boolean, temporal, UUID, and NULL values have explicit accounting; opaque/nested types fail closed with `cannot account for collected JDBC value` and a typed cause, before the row is retained. The new DuckDB nested-list rejection and existing UTF-8/binary boundary regressions passed **3/3**; complete `DuckFrameMetadataTest` passed **58/58**, zero failures/errors, in Docker. This is still a retained-result estimate, not a hard heap cap: JDBC materializes an individual value before Java can inspect its size. Worker-route inventory and aggregate budget coverage remain open.
- Job close idempotence (Docker, 2026-10-02): added `DuckRuntimeOwnershipTest.repeatedJobCloseReleasesExactlyOneConnectionSlot`, which invokes `DuckJob.close()` twice, proves another job can acquire the one configured slot, and verifies a third job remains blocked until the second closes. Focused test passed **1/1** and the full ownership suite passed **13/13** with no failures/errors.
